# Phase 7 spec 18 — The UHD Radio Module `ezsdr.radio.uhd` 0.4.0

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Accepted at Gate P (owner, 2026-09-27) and at Gate X (owner, 2026-10-01; [`plan/phase7/00-overview.md`](../plan/phase7/00-overview.md) §11), and moved here at Step X. Normative for `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd`. Its record — the design reviews J and K, the implementation reviews L–T, the bench session on an X300 + OBX and the design changes it caused — stays in [`plan/phase7/`](../plan/phase7/00-overview.md): [`design-notes.md`](../plan/phase7/design-notes.md) §6–§20 and [`bench-results.md`](../plan/phase7/bench-results.md). Its amendments, and the maintenance-spec 20 and 21 amendments before them, are recorded in the closing Changes table. |
| Scope | One Module with two roles — a Radio Provider of the `radio` Vocabulary 2.0.0 and a device-paced Time Authority — for one USRP X3xx whose daughterboards the binding's layout names per Run: its device boundary, its bridge to UHD, its profile `x3xx` 0.1.0, its Authority, its Provider's lifecycle, receive and transmit paths, its refusal of Run-time parameter updates, events and Manifest sections, its test double `FakeDevice`, and its hardware tests. |
| Not in scope | A second device per Run; a USRP2 profile; RFNoC Replay, DDC/DUC and FFT capabilities; `extensions.uhd.*`; GPIO and Peripherals (Vision §67's advanced peripherals, after the freeze); a transmit port (Phase 10) ([`00-overview.md`](../plan/phase7/00-overview.md) §3). |
| Crate | `crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd`, library `ezsdr_radio_uhd`. Depends on `ezsdr-kernel`, `ezsdr-radio`, `ezsdr-hostmem`, `serde_json`. Feature `uhd` (off by default) compiles `src/uhd.rs` and links `libuhd` (UR-35). |
| Depends on | Specs 01–07, 09 (both Providers' X3xx values are `ezsdr_radio::x3xx`'s; design 33 D5), 10, and spec 19's KG-1…KG-14 and VE-1…VE-3. |
| Modal verbs | "must" and "must not" are normative (OV-4a). |

---

## 1. Purpose

Vision §59 asks whether experiments written against MockRadio run on a USRP by changing only the BindingProfile. This Module is the other side of that comparison: it implements spec 07 on an X310 as MockRadio implements it in simulation, reports what the device does through the same events, flags and records, and declares what it cannot promise. It is written so that everything except the device itself runs without hardware: the Provider's logic talks to a `Device` trait, and the tests drive it with `FakeDevice`, a double of a USRP whose host clock is the real one by default and the manual one the smoke set selects (UR-2, UR-33), through the real coordinator.

## 2. Evidence

- **UHD's C API is a narrow bridge already** (VERIFIED, UHD 4.10 headers on this Mac, `/opt/homebrew/include`): every C entry point wraps its body in `UHD_SAFE_C`, which catches `uhd::exception`, `boost::exception`, `std::exception` and `...`, stores the text for `uhd_get_last_error` and returns a `uhd_error` code (`uhd/error.h:89-104`). Handles are opaque pointers, structs are POD (`uhd/types/metadata.h`, `tune_request.h`, `usrp/usrp.h`). This is what Vision §35 asks the bridge to be. **The functions and structs UR-2 and UR-3 add** — `uhd_subdev_spec_{make,free}`, `uhd_usrp_set_{rx,tx}_subdev_spec`, `uhd_meta_range_{make,free,size,at}`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_rates`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_antenna`, `uhd_usrp_get_rx_info` with `uhd_usrp_rx_info_free`, and `uhd_range_t` and `uhd_usrp_rx_info_t` — are declared in those headers, at the layouts `uhd_api_struct_sizes_match_the_headers` records (UHD 4.10.0's `usrp/usrp.h`, `types/ranges.h`, `types/usrp_info.h`; checked against the bench image's copy of the same headers).
- **Receive metadata codes** (VERIFIED, `uhd/types/metadata.h:80-92`): `NONE 0x0`, `TIMEOUT 0x1`, `LATE_COMMAND 0x2`, `BROKEN_CHAIN 0x4`, `OVERFLOW 0x8` (with `out_of_sequence` distinguishing a sequence error), `ALIGNMENT 0xC`, `BAD_PACKET 0xF`. **Asynchronous transmit codes** (`:232-244`): `BURST_ACK 0x1`, `UNDERFLOW 0x2`, `SEQ_ERROR 0x4`, `TIME_ERROR 0x8`, `UNDERFLOW_IN_PACKET 0x10`, `SEQ_ERROR_IN_BURST 0x20`, `USER_PAYLOAD 0x40`.
- **Timed commands** (VERIFIED, `uhd/usrp/multi_usrp.hpp`, `set_command_time`): "A timed command will back-pressure all subsequent timed commands … If the time spec is late, the command will be activated upon arrival." The device neither refuses a full queue nor reports a late command: the Provider counted and judged both itself (UR-24, withdrawn).
- **The DSP's timed frequency** (VERIFIED, UHD 4.10 FPGA and host source, `v4.10.0.0`): a timed `SR_FREQ` write to a DDC or DUC is queued and taken only by a sample that passes the block with a time at or after it, one command to a sample (`rfnoc_block_ddc.v:298-319`, `ddc.v:118`, `axi_tag_time.v:207-230`; the DDC counts input samples of the radio's rate, the DUC's timed DDS is after interpolation, `rfnoc_block_duc.v:291-330`). A DDC whose queue is full stops acknowledging its control port, untimed frequency writes included (bench shake-out 2); a DUC does not stop, and what an overflow leaves of the pairing of times and values is INFERRED. A DUC output carries a time until a packet without one begins and a later timed packet does not bring it back (`axi_rate_change.v:396-433`), and UHD times every fragment of a timed `send` and no fragment of an untimed one (`tx_streamer_impl.hpp:297-317`). The queues are in the FPGA and are emptied when the device is opened (`rfnoc_graph.cpp:714-722`); whether closing a streamer empties them is INFERRED (bench P4). The queue's capacity is INFERRED and unmeasured; the fake models no such queue (UR-33).
- **Setting device time** (VERIFIED, `set_time_unknown_pps`): "a 2-step process, and will take at most 2 seconds", and "when changing clock sources, a previously set time will most likely be lost … set the time after changing the clock source". v3 sets the clock source, then the time source, in that order (`v3/cpp/uhd_usrp/multiusrp.cpp:332-347`, cited by the spike).
- **Sensors** (VERIFIED, `uhd/usrp/usrp.h:448`, `uhd/types/sensors.h:55-121`): `uhd_usrp_get_mboard_sensor` and `uhd_sensor_value_to_bool` read `ref_locked`.
- **Alignment** (VERIFIED by Phase 4 Review D in UHD master `0d7ed3b`, `rx_streamer_impl.hpp`, `get_aligned_buffs.hpp`): an alignment failure yields no samples, or only aligned ones, and discards packets whose timestamps disagree; never a partial channel set.
- **Overflow**: UHD restarts a continuous stream `OVERRUN_RESTART_DELAY` = 0.05 s after an overflow (VERIFIED, audit Finding 3; MR-3).
- **The X3xx values** of `ezsdr_radio::x3xx`'s description, which both Providers build (design 33 D5), with the sources MR-3 marks (spec 09 §2).
- **The spike** (`spike/uhd`, VERIFIED on its fake device and against libuhd with no device): the C API slice of ~40 functions, the anchor-and-extrapolate Authority, the thread structure, and findings K1–K13.
- **INFERRED** (checked on the bench, `bench.md`): the device lead, the delivery allowance, the receive start lead and the in-flight window the profile declares; which UHD errors mean a lost device (UR-29); the drift bounds of UR-8; and every INFERRED value of UR-9. The timed-command queue depth (UR-24, withdrawn) was measured on the bench until note 48 S2a-i-α removed its probe, and its key left in note 48 S2b-i. **Measured on the bench and applied** (design-notes §11, the owner's decision of 2026-09-30): the X3x0 ignores the time of a continuous receive stream's stop and stops at once (VERIFIED: UHD 4.10's `radio_rx_core.v` and the bench's timed-stop probe, removed by note 48 S2a-i-β), so UR-25 hands the device no timed stop; the X300 drops a timed start-of-burst at the tick its previous burst ended (`hw_b8_raw_burst_gap`), so UR-23 continues the device burst there.

## 3. Model

```text
                         runtime (server catalogue / test assembler)
                               │ open(args) ──► Arc<dyn Device> ◄── FakeDevice::new(..) (tests only)
               ┌───────────────┴────────────────┐
         DeviceAuthority (Authority)        UhdRadio (Provider, not stepped)
         root = device master clock           │ prepare: apply, verify, declare clocks
         now = anchor + host elapsed          │ arm: streamers, transmit clock on the lattice
         uhd-clock: re-anchor every 100 ms    │ start(T0): receive clock at T0, timed start
         next_wakeup: nap ≤ 20 ms             │
                                              │
            threads:  uhd-control ── ActionReceiver (MA-14b loop, 1 ms): books Actions,
                                     checks reference and device time
                      uhd-rx-s<k> ── one per declared receive stream (design 33 D15, D4), owning
                                     that stream's streamer: recv → blocks → its port's links;
                                     RX_OVERFLOW on the hot path; idle
                                     while its stream holds no channel
                      uhd-tx-s<k> ── one per declared transmit stream, owning that stream's
                                     streamer: bursts in target order → send, BurstTracker;
                                     async reports; idle while its stream
                                     holds no channel

 Kernel side (spec 19): data thread steps the capture Sink (KC-46); KC-21a waits for uhd-control to
 finish a Session's Actions; a DEVICE_LOST from a Provider thread ends the streams at once (MA-9a, KC-46b).
```

A layout instance declares one stream per channel in each direction (design 33 D4) and starts a thread for each whether or not it holds a channel.

A tick of the Run's primary root is one master-clock tick of the device (200 MHz). Every SampleClock is `root_ticks_per_tick = N` for the device's integer decimation `N` (UR-12).

The Module's own timing values, all INFERRED and measured at bench step B8, are: the **device lead** 2 ms (the lead it needs from receipt, UR-21); the **delivery allowance** 3 ms (UR-14; the declared lead is their sum, 5 ms); the **receive start lead**, 50 ms, the profile's `radio.timing.rx_start_lead_ns` (UR-9, UR-25), whose value the bench measures (a transmit line carries none); and the **in-flight window**, the profile's `radio.timing.tx_in_flight_ns`, 10 ms on `x3xx` (UR-9, UR-23). It is a strict payload bound: the first sample at or after `now + W` is not handed over. **Ceiling:** the receive start lead is 50 ms, the value of the constant it replaces, so no test tells the Module reading its profile from reading a constant until the bench measures them.

## 4. Rules

### Identity and the device boundary

- **UR-1** The Module is `ModuleDescriptor { id: ezsdr.radio.uhd, version: 0.4.0, kernel_api: 4.0.0, roles: [Provider, Authority], vocabularies: [{ radio, ^2.0.0 }], impl_hash: Some(ContentHash::of_bytes(b"ezsdr.radio.uhd 0.4.0")) }`, returned by `ezsdr_radio_uhd::descriptor()`; `module_ref()` returns its `{ id, version }`. A runtime registers it with `Factories { provider: true, authority: true }`.
  *Test:* `ur_01_the_descriptor_registers` (the roles, the impl hash and version 0.4.0, and the refusal of a runtime whose `radio` Vocabulary is not 2.0.0).
- **UR-2** Everything the Provider and the Authority do to a device goes through the object-safe trait `ezsdr_radio_uhd::Device: Send + Sync`, whose methods are exactly:

  ```rust
  fn describe(&self) -> serde_json::Value;           // identity for the Manifest (UR-30)
  fn fidelity(&self) -> Fidelity;                    // UR-31
  fn master_clock_rate(&self) -> u64;                // integer Hz
  fn channels(&self, dir: Dir) -> usize;             // Dir::{Rx, Tx}: the direction's current front ends
  fn front_end(&self, dir: Dir, chan: usize) -> Result<String, DeviceError>;  // UHD's name, e.g. "UBX RX" (UR-5)
  fn serial(&self) -> Result<String, DeviceError>;   // the motherboard's, design 33 D5
  fn set_subdev_spec(&self, dir: Dir, markup: &str) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;  // UHD's markup, e.g. "A:0 B:0"
  fn rates(&self, dir: Dir, chan: usize) -> Result<Vec<(f64, f64, f64)>, DeviceError>;  // UHD's meta range, each (start, stop, step)
  fn set_sources(&self, clock: &str, time: &str) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;
  fn set_time_zero(&self, at_next_pps: bool) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;
  fn time_now(&self) -> Result<i64, DeviceError>;    // master-clock ticks since the time was set
  fn ref_locked(&self) -> Result<Option<bool>, DeviceError>;  // None: the device has no such sensor
  fn apply(&self, dir: Dir, chan: usize, s: &Settings) -> Result<Applied, DeviceError>;
  fn rx_open(&self, stream: usize, channels: &[usize]) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;  // stream from 0 per direction; the channels are the device's own
  fn rx_start(&self, stream: usize, at: i64) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;
  fn rx_packet_samples(&self, stream: usize) -> usize;  // UHD's max_num_samps; 0 when that streamer is not open (UR-25)
  fn rx_stop(&self, stream: usize) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;
  fn rx_recv(&self, stream: usize, n: usize, timeout: std::time::Duration) -> RxRecv;
  fn tx_open(&self, stream: usize, channels: &[usize]) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;
  fn tx_send(&self, stream: usize, samples: &[&[Iq]], at: Option<i64>, sob: bool, eob: bool,
             timeout: std::time::Duration) -> Result<usize, DeviceError>;
  fn tx_async(&self, stream: usize, timeout: std::time::Duration) -> Result<Option<TxReport>, DeviceError>;
  fn close_streams(&self);                           // every streamer of both directions (UR-16)
  fn mark_lost(&self);
  fn raise_thread_priority(&self) -> Result<(), DeviceError>;  // the calling thread to real-time priority (UR-23)
  fn host(&self) -> HostClock;                       // the real host clock, or a manual one (UR-33)
  ```

  with `Iq = [f32; 2]` (UHD's `fc32`); `Settings { rate, freq, gain: Option<f64>, antenna: Option<String> }`, `None` leaving a setting alone; `Applied { rate, freq, gain: f64, antenna: Option<String> }`, read back after an untimed change, the antenna being the one the channel holds when the change named one and `None` otherwise (design 33 D16); `DeviceError { lost: bool, message: String }`, `lost` meaning the device is gone (UR-29); `RxRecv` one of `Samples { first_tick: i64, samples: Vec<Vec<Iq>> }` (one vector per channel, all the same length ≥ 1), `Overflow { out_of_sequence: bool }`, `LateCommand`, `Alignment`, `BadPacket`, `Timeout` and `Failed(DeviceError)`; `TxReport { code: TxCode, tick: Option<i64>, channel: usize }` with `TxCode` one of `BurstAck`, `Underflow`, `UnderflowInPacket`, `SeqError`, `SeqErrorInBurst`, `TimeError`, `Other(i32)`. Ticks are master-clock ticks; no floating-point time crosses the trait. Streams are numbered from 0 in each direction, and a call's channels are the device's own numbers under its current subdev spec (UR-3, design 33 D5).
  *Test:* `ur_02_device_is_object_safe_and_shared` (the trait is object-safe and `Send + Sync`, and a `FakeDevice` is reached as `Arc<dyn Device>`); the added methods' signatures and behaviour on the fake by `ur_33_the_front_end_table_follows_the_subdev_spec`, `ur_33_a_subdev_spec_uhd_would_refuse_is_refused`, `ur_33_rates_report_the_grid_and_the_pre_chain_one`, `ur_33_the_serial_is_the_config_s`, `ur_33_two_receive_streamers_keep_their_own_channels`, `ur_33_two_transmit_streamers_keep_their_own_reports`, `ur_33_one_channel_is_opened_by_one_streamer` and `ur_33_the_antenna_is_read_back_after_an_untimed_change`; none named for each other method's signature.
  The call's behaviour: `ur_23_p1_p2_each_owner_raises_its_own_thread_once_before_its_first_send` and `ur_23_g2_every_wrapper_forwards_the_priority_call` (the measurement's guard, counting and probe wrappers forward it; a latched guard refuses it, `ur_23_g3_…`; the test doubles answer it themselves).
- **UR-3** `UhdDevice` (module `src/uhd.rs`, feature `uhd`) implements `Device` on UHD's C API, and is the crate's only `unsafe` code. It declares by hand exactly the functions it calls, all present in UHD 4.10.0's headers:
  - `uhd_get_last_error`, `uhd_usrp_last_error`;
  - `uhd_set_thread_priority`;
  - `uhd_string_vector_{make,free,size,at}`;
  - `uhd_usrp_{find,make,free,get_pp_string,get_master_clock_rate,get_time_now,set_time_now,set_time_unknown_pps,set_command_time,clear_command_time,set_clock_source,set_time_source,get_mboard_sensor,get_mboard_sensor_names}`;
  - `uhd_sensor_value_{make,free,to_bool}`;
  - `uhd_subdev_spec_{make,free}`, `uhd_usrp_set_{rx,tx}_subdev_spec`;
  - `uhd_meta_range_{make,free,size,at}`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_rates`;
  - `uhd_usrp_get_rx_info`, `uhd_usrp_rx_info_free`;
  - `uhd_usrp_{get,set}_{rx,tx}_{rate,gain}`, `uhd_usrp_set_{rx,tx}_freq`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_freq`, `uhd_usrp_set_{rx,tx}_antenna`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_antenna`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_num_channels`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_subdev_name`;
  - `uhd_{rx,tx}_streamer_{make,free,max_num_samps,last_error}`, `uhd_usrp_get_{rx,tx}_stream`, `uhd_rx_streamer_recv`, `uhd_rx_streamer_issue_stream_cmd`, `uhd_tx_streamer_send`, `uhd_tx_streamer_recv_async_msg`;
  - `uhd_rx_metadata_{make,free,has_time_spec,time_spec,error_code,out_of_sequence,strerror}`;
  - `uhd_tx_metadata_{make,free}`;
  - `uhd_async_metadata_{make,free,channel,has_time_spec,time_spec,event_code}`.

  These are used with the structs `uhd_stream_args_t`, `uhd_stream_cmd_t`, `uhd_tune_request_t`, `uhd_tune_result_t`, `uhd_range_t` and `uhd_usrp_rx_info_t`, laid out as the UHD 4.x headers lay them out. Every call's `uhd_error` other than `UHD_ERROR_NONE` becomes `DeviceError { lost, message: "<call>: UHD error <code>: <text>" }`, the text read from the handle the call was made on — `uhd_usrp_last_error` under the control-call mutex, `uhd_{rx,tx}_streamer_last_error` under the streamer's call mutex, a stream open's from the streamer's handle, where UHD saves it — and from `uhd_get_last_error` for a call on no usrp or streamer handle (the meta-range and subdev-spec calls included). A streamer value owns its handles from their creation and is moved into its `Arc`, and an open releases that stream's old streamer before it makes the new one, so a refused reopen leaves the stream with none. A call naming a stream no streamer is open for fails at once, and `tx_async` finds no report at once. `set_subdev_spec` frees its spec handle whether `uhd_subdev_spec_make` succeeded or not (`subdev_spec_c.cpp:31-36`). Streams are `fc32` on the host and `sc16` on the wire, so `radio.perf.wire_bytes_per_sample` is UR-9's. `serial` is `uhd_usrp_get_rx_info`'s `mboard_serial` of channel 0 (INFERRED).

  **Time conversion** (TM-20): a tick `t` is sent as `full = t.div_euclid(mcr)`, `frac = t.rem_euclid(mcr) as f64 / mcr as f64`; a time spec `(full, frac)` is read as `full · mcr + round(frac · mcr)`, rounding to nearest, ties away from zero, with `checked` arithmetic (an overflow is a `DeviceError`); a tick converted to a time spec and back is the same tick for every tick in `0 ..= 2^53` (`rem < mcr < 2^28`, so the round trip is exact). `open` refuses a device whose master clock rate is not a positive whole number of hertz.

  **Threads.** The handles are shared by the Module's threads under two rules: every **control call** (`apply`; `set_subdev_spec`, `rates` and `serial`; `set_sources`, `set_time_zero`, `time_now`, `ref_locked`; and the timed stream commands of UR-15, UR-17 and UR-25) is made under one `UhdDevice` mutex, and a time read used for an anchor or a relation takes its host brackets around `Device::time_now`, which takes the mutex inside, so that a read that waited for the mutex is widened and UR-7's 1 ms rule discards it (the `Device` trait has no bracketed read);

  and each **streamer** has one owning thread at a time — its uhd-rx thread owns a receive one (`recv`, the stream commands, and a reopen, UR-25) and its uhd-tx a transmit one (`send`, `recv_async_msg`, and a reopen; design 33 D15) — except for the stream an enable from no stream gives uhd-control until its booking, or until the enable ends without one, and the report read the transmit shutdown makes after the join bound left uhd-control detached (UR-25, UR-16) — each kept alive by an `Arc` its owner holds, so that `close_streams` frees only a streamer no thread holds (UR-16). The safe `Device` methods enforce exclusive native access with a separate mutex on each streamer, held through the call and all metadata/error-text reads; receive stream commands share that receive streamer's mutex, and transmit sends and asynchronous reports share their transmit streamer's. Construction and final drop have exclusive handle ownership. `UhdDevice` is `Send + Sync` on that enforced basis, stated at its `unsafe impl` (INFERRED from UHD's documented `multi_usrp` thread safety).

  *Test:* `ur_03_time_specs_round_trip` (the conversion functions live outside `uhd.rs`, so the default build runs it: the tick round trip at `0`, `1`, `199 999 999`, `200 000 000`, `2^53` and `−1`, and the overflow refusal), and with the feature `uhd_api_find_returns`, `uhd_api_open_of_an_absent_device_is_an_error_with_uhd_s_text` (the `<call>: UHD error …` text), `uhd_api_struct_sizes_match_the_headers`, `uhd_api_a_streamer_is_freed_once` and `uhd_api_a_refused_subdev_spec_is_freed_once` (against real libuhd: the refused markup's own call name and UHD's text, while the free itself has no witness there), and `uhd_a_subdev_spec_is_freed_once_on_every_path` (feature `uhd`, in `src/uhd.rs`, whose make, set and free calls are replaced for that test binary: one free per path — after the set, the handle a refused make had allocated before it parsed, and a failed set's), and `uhd_streams_are_looked_up_by_number` (the streamer map, a call on a number with no streamer, and each streamer's own channel count and packet size) and `uhd_a_failed_streamer_open_reads_its_text_from_the_streamer` (a refused `uhd_usrp_get_rx_stream` and `uhd_usrp_get_tx_stream` carry the streamer's text, not the device's); none named for the declared-function list, the other calls' last-error choice, the mutex and control-call rules, the wire format or the master-clock refusal.
  `uhd_set_thread_priority`: `ur_23_f1_the_priority_call_is_uhd_s_realtime_half` and `ur_23_f2_every_refusal_is_an_error_naming_the_call_and_the_code` (the feature `uhd`'s unit tests; every other test of that module still links with the symbol's shim).
- **UR-4** `ezsdr_radio_uhd::open(args: &str) -> Result<Arc<dyn Device>, String>` opens the device UHD's device string `args` names (`uhd_usrp_make`). Built without the feature `uhd` it returns `Err("ezsdr.radio.uhd: built without UHD (feature `uhd`)")`. It never returns a `FakeDevice`, whatever `args` says. A runtime calls it once per binding, and the Provider and the Authority built from that binding share the returned object (UR-6).
  *Test:* `ur_04_open_without_the_feature_refuses` (the build without the feature) and `ur_33_no_document_selects_the_fake_device` (`open("fake")` and `open("type=fake")` refuse, and the selector key `fake` is refused).

### Construction and the Authority

- **UR-5** `UhdRadio::from_binding(binding: &Binding, device: Arc<dyn Device>) -> Result<UhdRadio, ModuleError>` refuses with `Rejected`: a `module` other than UR-1's; a `profile` other than `x3xx` 0.1.0 (absent is refused); a `feed`; a selector key other than the ones below, or one of the wrong kind or out of range; every refusal `ezsdr_radio::channels::from_selector` makes of the shared keys (a missing, malformed or repeated-pair layout among them, and a bad or repeated `#<name>` after an entry; design 33 D5, RM-28); a `serial()` that fails, and a device whose serial a live `UhdRadio` still holds (reason naming it); a device whose master clock rate is not the profile's master clock (UR-9) (reason `"UR-5: profile x3xx needs a 200 MHz master clock; the device runs at <n> Hz"`); and a device whose front end on one of the layout's channels `Device::front_end` names otherwise than that element's board (`UBX…`, `OBX…` or `CBX…`, as UHD names them: `UBX RX`, `OBX TX`, `CBX-120 TX`; reason `"UR-5: the layout's <rx|tx> channel <c> is `<name>`, not `<board> front end <n>`"`). The layout decides the channels and the boards; the module-specific keys are the Module's own (UR-9).
 The opening checks run in one order: `serial()`, registered for the instance's life (UR-5, design 33 D5); the master clock; each direction's subdev spec — `<slot>:<front end>` per element in its order, one ASCII space apart, and none for a direction with no element (H2); then `front_end` on the layout's channels, whose names are checked against the elements (a Basic/LF board down to its front end's number, `BasicRX (1)`). A `set_subdev_spec` the device refuses is refused with UHD's text — a device with fewer channels than the layout names included —, and a spec is set before any front end is read (H1).

  UHD clips a tune to the front end's range widened by the DSP's reach (min(DSP range, bandwidth / 2), `multi_usrp_utils.hpp`) without a warning and reports it in `tune_result.clipped_rf_freq` and the read-back, which UR-12 records but does not refuse. A real X310 with one CBX in slot A and slot B empty reports 2 + 2 channels, channel 0 the CBX and channel 1 UHD's unknown board (`db_unknown.cpp`), so only the layout's own channels are checked:

  | Selector key | Kind | Default | Meaning |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `rx`, `tx` | list of str, each `"<timekeeper>:<slot>:<board>:<frontend>"`, in the channel order, any slot order (no repeated `(slot, front end)` pair) | — (required) | the channel layout: its length is that direction's channel count (0 when the list is empty); shared with MockRadio and parsed by `ezsdr_radio::channels::from_selector` (design 33 D5) |
  | `master_clock_hz` | list of one int | `[200000000]` | the master clock; 200 MHz is the only accepted value (UR-9) |
  | `image` | list of one str: `hg` or `xg` | `["hg"]` | the FPGA image; checked and used by nothing yet (design 33 D5) |
  | `links` | list of one int, 1 or 2 | `[1]` | the number of 10 GbE links; checked and used by nothing yet (design 33 D5, D9) |
  | `link_gbps` | list of one int, 1 or 10 | `[1]` with `hg`, `[10]` with `xg` | the speed of one link, which decides the transport |
  | `args` | str, required | — | UHD's device string; read by the runtime's `open` (UR-4) and recorded (UR-30) |
  | `id` | str, one `ResourceId` segment | `"usrp"` | the device node's path |
  | `clock_source` | str: `internal`, `external` or `gpsdo` | `internal` | the 10 MHz reference (UR-7) |
  | `time_source` | str: `internal`, `external` or `gpsdo` | `internal` | the PPS source (UR-7) |
  | `block_len` | int, 1…65 536 | the profile's `radio.rx.block_len` (UR-9) | samples per receive `recv` and nominal block (`radio.rx.block_len`); a Provider-interpreted hint (Vision §33) |
  | `reopen_on_unlock` | bool | `true` | whether a builder that may open the device again does so once when the reference PLL did not lock (UR-7; the server's `assemble`) |

  *Test:* `ur_05_from_binding_refusals` (the module, an absent or foreign profile, a `feed`, the selector keys, a malformed shared selector and an `id` of `""` or `a/b`, and the master-clock reason), `ur_05_keys_is_an_unknown_selector_key`, `ur_05_the_profile_must_be_the_device_s_front_ends` (the front-end refusals, with their reason strings, a layout mixing two board kinds, and a device with fewer channels than the layout names refused by the spec itself, with UHD's text) and `ur_05_the_layout_builds_the_subdev_spec`, `ur_05_the_layout_s_order_is_the_device_s_channel_order`, `ur_05_a_direction_without_elements_sets_no_spec`, `ur_05_the_spec_is_set_before_the_channel_map_is_read`, `ur_05_the_front_end_check_reads_the_device_after_the_subdev_spec`, `ur_05_a_board_that_differs_from_the_layout_is_refused`, `ur_05_a_basic_front_end_s_number_is_checked`, `ur_05_a_subdev_spec_the_device_refuses_is_refused_with_uhd_s_text` and `ur_05_each_channel_is_checked_at_its_own_device_channel` (the opening checks' order, the markup and its order, the empty direction, the front ends' names and numbers, and UHD's text); the serial's one live instance by `ur_05_a_second_binding_of_one_serial_is_refused`, `ur_05_a_parallel_bind_of_one_serial_succeeds_once`,
`ur_05_a_failed_binding_leaves_no_serial_registered`, `ur_05_a_serial_read_that_fails_refuses`, `ur_05_a_radio_dropped_after_its_device_is_lost_releases_its_serial`, `ur_05_cleanup_does_not_release_the_serial` (a live instance keeps it after `cleanup`, its drop releases it) and `ur_05_two_default_fakes_bind_together`; the `id` default `"usrp"` by `ur_10_instance` and the `reopen_on_unlock` default by `ea_07_a_reference_that_locks_on_the_reopen_connects` (a first open whose reference does not lock reopens once, by default, and the Run records it, its serial one device's: `ea_07_the_reopen_of_one_device_is_not_a_second_binding`); none named for the table's `clock_source` and `time_source` defaults.
- **UR-6** The Authority role is taken by the radio's own binding: the BindingProfile's `authority` names the binding of `ezsdr.radio.uhd`, and the runtime builds `DeviceAuthority::new(device, clocks, clock_source, time_source, args)` from it, with the same device object as the Provider (SB-22c). The Provider refuses in `prepare` a primary root that is not its own device's: the root's epoch must be the one UR-7 registers for the Provider's own `clock_source`, `time_source` and `args` (reason `"UR-6: the primary root is not this device's; bind the Authority to the radio's own binding"`).
  *Test:* `ur_06_the_provider_refuses_another_root` (an `internal`/`internal`, an `internal`/`external` and an `external`/`external` Authority's root, each refused at `prepare` with a reason beginning `"UR-6: the primary root is not this device's"`); none named for the rest of the reason.
- **UR-7** `DeviceAuthority` implements `Authority` with pacing `Device`. `new` sets the reference sources (`set_sources(clock_source, time_source)`, the clock source first), then sets the device's time to zero — at the next PPS edge (`set_time_zero(true)`, up to 2 s) when `time_source` is not `internal`, at once otherwise — and registers the device's master clock as a new Root with rate `master_clock_rate / 1` and its epoch (TM-3): `Pps { set_by: "ezsdr.radio.uhd.set_time_unknown_pps:<args>" }` when neither source is `internal`, so that the master clock is locked to the PPS's reference and every whole second of the root is a PPS edge; `Arbitrary` with that `set_by` when only `time_source` is not `internal`; and `Arbitrary { set_by: "ezsdr.radio.uhd.set_time_now:<args>" }` otherwise (INFERRED: UHD counts master-clock ticks after `set_time_unknown_pps`). Its `TimeAuthority`:
  - `now(root)` extrapolates from an **anchor** (a host `Instant` and a device tick read together) by the host time elapsed since, and is monotonic (a value never below one it returned before). The anchor is re-read from the device by a thread of the Authority's own, `uhd-clock`, every 100 ms for as long as the Authority lives, and at every nap of `next_wakeup` and `wait_until` — a read whose host bracket is wider than 1 ms is discarded and the previous anchor kept —, so the extrapolation never runs on the host clock alone for more than 100 ms, whether or not a client call is running; the anchor records the read in the host clock's time (the device's `HostClock`, real or manual: UR-2, UR-33) and its bracket rule runs on that clock; with the manual clock only a read during which that clock did not move sets the anchor, the first included: `uhd-clock` reads the device as it starts, before its first wait, and `new` returns only once that read was exact,
    refusing the Authority when it was not or did not come within 5 s (`new`'s own read, made while the clock runs, then serves only UR-8's relations; on the real clock it is still the first anchor and nothing waits),
    so the clock's own rules M1–M7 of UR-33 hold for the waits that read it (a stopped clock blocks them: they do not poll); `now(host.monotonic)` is the nanoseconds since the Authority was built — real time, never the manual clock — and a `host.monotonic` `wait_until`, `schedule` or `next_wakeup` is judged on that real time, never converted to root ticks, in both clock modes; the root and its derived domains share one queue and `last_fired` and `host.monotonic` another; `now` of a domain derived from the root is the floor conversion (TM-16c);
  - `schedule(t, f)` and `cancel` are TM-16c's and MA-29's (spec 01, spec 05): the refusals, the order callbacks fire in, the separation of the root's timeline from `host.monotonic`'s, and, for `cancel`, what it reports and the wait it wakes;
  - `next_wakeup()` waits in naps of at most 20 ms, each followed by a re-anchor, woken early by `schedule` and `cancel`, after which it re-evaluates (returning `None` if a cancel emptied the schedule), and returns the instant it reached, the HOST time for a `host.monotonic` one (TM-16c, TM-17b, MA-29);
  - `wait_until(t)` sleeps the same way until `now` reaches `t`, judged in `t`'s own domain: a root instant on the device's host clock (a clock sleep, which blocks while the manual clock is stopped) and a `host.monotonic` one on real time;
  - it governs its root, the root's derived domains and `host.monotonic` (TM-16a, TM-16a1), and its descriptor is `{ module: module_ref(), governs: [root, host.monotonic], pacing: Device }`.

  A device time read that fails, or that is bracketed more widely than 1 ms, leaves the anchor as it was, so `now` goes on advancing with the device's host clock (real or manual, UR-33) from the last good read; both are counted, by `DeviceAuthority::failed_reads()` and `discarded_reads()`, not in the Manifest (an Authority has no section of its own, MA-29); a lost device is found and reported by the Provider (UR-29), not by the Authority.

  A `set_clock_source` that fails because the X300's reference PLL did not lock within UHD's 30 s (`x300_mb_controller.cpp:243–255`) refuses the Authority with `UR-7: the reference clock did not lock to its <clock_source> source within UHD's 30 s; connecting again usually succeeds (<UHD's text>)`, which the server returns as the Connect's refusal; the error begins with `REFERENCE_DID_NOT_LOCK`, which the crate exports. **The server opens the device again once on it** — dropping the first device and its radio, building both anew — unless the binding's `reopen_on_unlock` is `false`; a second refusal stands, and a Run built on the reopen records `timing { reopened_on_unlock, first_error }` (INFERRED that an open within the same process locks as a new process did). The hardware tests build the Authority directly and do not reopen.

  `uhd-clock` ends when the Authority is dropped, and `drop` waits for it: it wakes the thread at once and the thread checks its stop flag before each read, so that no device read follows `drop` and the last device handle is never dropped on `uhd-clock`; a read still blocked inside UHD after 1 s (a device gone, which the Provider reports) leaves the thread detached.

  *Test:* `ur_07_a_reference_that_does_not_lock_is_named` (the reason and UHD's text), the server's `ea_07_a_reference_that_does_not_lock_refuses_the_connect_with_its_reason`, `ea_07_a_reference_that_locks_on_the_reopen_connects` and `ea_07_the_reopen_can_be_switched_off`, `ur_07_the_root_s_epoch_is_the_pps_edge_with_external_sources` (the epoch and `set_by` of each source branch — both sources not `internal`, only the time source not `internal`, otherwise), `ur_07_the_last_device_handle_is_not_dropped_on_uhd_clock`, `ur_07_no_device_read_follows_the_authority_s_drop`, `ur_07_the_authority_paces_to_the_device`, `ur_07_schedule_wakes_a_waiting_next_wakeup`, `ur_07_schedule_accepts_an_instant_already_passed`, `ur_07_cancel_wakes_a_waiting_next_wakeup`, `ur_07_now_is_monotonic_across_a_reanchor`, `ur_07_now_tracks_the_device_while_no_call_runs` and `ur_07_the_time_is_set_at_the_next_pps_with_an_external_source`; in `src/authority.rs`, `ur_07_a_read_bracketed_wider_than_1_ms_is_discarded` (which also checks `discarded_reads()`), `ur_07_the_first_anchor_is_exact_on_the_manual_clock`, `ur_07_uhd_clock_re_anchors_follow_a_drifting_device`, `ur_07_a_first_read_that_is_not_exact_refuses_the_authority`, `ur_07_the_root_fires_first_when_both_are_due` and `ur_07_in_past_is_judged_per_root_timeline` (the root's and `host.monotonic`'s separate timelines, unit); none named for `failed_reads`.
- **UR-8** `DeviceAuthority::relations()` returns the two relations measured in `new` right after the time was set (TM-18). One device time read `t` is bracketed by host reads: the host's monotonic clock and its UTC, each read immediately before and after it. **To `host.monotonic`**: `ClockRelation { source: root, target: host.monotonic, measured_at: t, offset: the midpoint of the two monotonic reads, drift: 0.0, drift_uncertainty: 1e-4, uncertainty: the interval between them, method: "ezsdr.radio.uhd.host_bracket", valid: { from: t, to: None } }` — on the real clock the same pair of reads the anchor of UR-7 starts from; on the manual clock UR-7's first anchor is `uhd-clock`'s own exact read, and this one serves the relations only.

  With the manual clock (UR-33) the device's time stands still while the host and UTC go on, so both relations' validity is the instant measured only, `{ from: t, to: Some(t) }`; with the real clock it is `{ from: t, to: None }` as above. **To `utc`**: the same with the UTC reads, `drift_uncertainty: 1e-5` and `method: "ezsdr.radio.uhd.host_utc_bracket"`; its `uncertainty` excludes the host clock's own error against UTC, which the Module cannot know (TM-18).

  The drift bounds are INFERRED.

  *Test:* `ur_08_the_relations_bracket_a_device_read` (the two targets, each relation's `source` the root, each `uncertainty` 0…10 ms, and the two `method` strings); in `src/authority.rs`, `ur_08_the_manual_clock_s_relations_hold_only_at_the_instant_measured` (both relations `{ from: t, to: Some(t) }` on the manual clock and `{ from: t, to: None }` on the real one, unit) and `ur_08_the_host_domain_follows_real_time_across_a_busy_interval` (unit); none named for the drift bounds.

### The profile

- **UR-9** The Module uses `ezsdr_radio::channels::Description` built from the binding's layout with the `x3xx` 0.1.0 terms from `ezsdr_radio::x3xx` (design 33 D5): the master clock, the rate grid, each board's frequency, gain, antennas and phase behaviour, the receive start lead and the rest of the timing envelope other than the command lead, the transport and the path delays are the Vocabulary's. The layout's boards decide the board-dependent values: `ubx` 10 000 000…6 000 000 000 Hz, `obx` 10 000 000…8 400 000 000 Hz (`obx_freq_range`, `db_obx.hpp`, VERIFIED) and `cbx` 1 200 000 000…6 000 000 000 Hz (`cbx_freq_range`, `db_sbx_common.hpp`, VERIFIED), each with gain 0…31.5 dB in 0.5 dB steps, antennas `RX2`/`TX/RX` for receive and `TX/RX` for transmit (`CAL` not offered), and a default frequency of 1 GHz except a CBX's 2 450 000 000 Hz. A board's phase behaviour is `random_unless_timed_tune` for a UBX and an OBX (`db_ubx.cpp`, `obx/obx_expert.cpp`, `_sync_phase`, VERIFIED) and `random` for a CBX. The description declares phase behavior per channel and makes no direction-wide coherence claim. Every value INFERRED stays INFERRED.

  Only these values are the Module's:

  | Value | `x3xx` 0.1.0 | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | `min_timed_command_lead_ns` | 5 000 000 | INFERRED: §3's device lead plus §3's delivery allowance (§3; RM-6) |
  | `radio.tx.repeat_max_samples`, `radio.tx.repeat_align_samples` | 4 294 967 295, 1 | this Module: the repeat is streamed from host memory (UR-22), so neither Replay's size nor its alignment applies |

  The description's own notes on the shared values: its master clock is the one UR-5 refuses another of; `radio.rx.block_len` is the selector's `block_len` when it is given (UR-5) and 2 000 otherwise; the device's DSP resolution is finer than the frequency step (`mcr / 2^32` ≈ 0.047 Hz), so an applied frequency may differ from the claim by less than 0.05 Hz, which UR-12 records (INFERRED); `startup_latency_ns` is kept for parity with MR-3, since `start` refuses a T0 less than it after `arm` (UR-15); `wire_bytes_per_sample` is `sc16` on the wire (UR-3); and the transport is one link's, whatever `links` says (design 33 D9). The Module always presents `ezsdr_radio::channels::Description` built from the binding's layout (RM-27, RM-29): one stream node per channel and direction (`<id>/rx/s<k>`, `<id>/tx/s<k>`), one receive port per channel (`rx<k>`), and RM-30's numbered configuration keys.

  No timed setting reaches the device, so no command-queue depth bounds the Module: a timed receive start goes through the radio's receive-command FIFO, which a STOP bypasses and clears, and a transmit timestamp travels in its data packet (UHD `radio_control_impl.cpp`, FPGA `radio_rx_core.v`, v4.10.0.0; design note 48 A2).

  UHD clips a tune outside a front end's range without a warning, which UR-12 records but does not refuse; the layout's channel count is what the device must have, not its full channel count (a slot B left empty is a channel on UHD's unknown board, `db_unknown.cpp`). UHD drives an OBX from 4.9 on (`db_obx.cpp` is absent from v4.8.0.0, VERIFIED); an older UHD names it an unknown board, which UR-5 refuses.
  *Test:* `ur_09_profile_values_reach_the_capabilities_and_the_envelope_section` (the capabilities for the lead, the startup latency, the repeat constraints, `radio.rx.block_len`, `wire_bytes_per_sample` and the transmit path delay, and the `envelope` section's profile, lead and receive transport);
  the layout's channel count by `ur_05_the_profile_must_be_the_device_s_front_ends`; the layout description (the tree, ports and numbered keys) by `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_declares_its_clock_and_opens_its_channels`, `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_applies_each_channel_s_own_settings` and `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_with_no_channel_declares_no_clock`; the board's frequency range, channel membership, phase behavior, absent direction-wide coherence claim and default by `ur_09_the_obx_is_one_channel_from_10_mhz_to_8_4_ghz_defaulting_to_1_ghz` and `ur_09_the_cbx_is_one_channel_from_1_2_ghz_defaulting_to_2_45_ghz`; none named for the gain and antenna values or for the notes.

### The Provider's lifecycle

- **UR-10** `instance()` returns `ProviderInstance { id: <id>, module: module_ref(), profile: the binding's, tree: the description's tree for <id> (RM-2, RM-29: one stream node per channel and direction and one receive port per channel), fidelity: device.fidelity() (UR-31), driving: { stepped: false }, arm_after: [], min_command_lead: Some(Duration(host.monotonic, UR-9's `min_timed_command_lead_ns`)), sections: UR-30's }`.
  *Test:* `ur_10_instance` (the profile, the id, `stepped: false`, an empty `arm_after`, the `host.monotonic` lead of UR-9's `min_timed_command_lead_ns` and the device node's host memory domain); the layout's tree and ports by `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_declares_its_clock_and_opens_its_channels` and `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_with_no_channel_declares_no_clock`.
- **UR-11** `coerce(request)` is the layout description's `coerce` for `<id>` (RM-29): pure, no device call (MA-11).
  *Test:* `ur_11_coerce_is_the_descriptions` (19.5 Msps → 20 Msps with one coercion; 7 GHz rejected; `radio.rx.c2.frequency_hz` refused); none named for `coerce` making no device call (MA-11).
- **UR-12** `prepare(fragment, ctx)` takes its device node from `ctx.source`, the node the Kernel names as its source root (KC-8; it is `<id>`), and emits under it and the nodes below it (MA-5); a `source` that is not a node is `Rejected`. It refuses with `Unsupported` a class other than HardwareInLoop or Hardware (reason `"UR-12: ezsdr.radio.uhd runs HardwareInLoop or Hardware, not <class>"`), and with `Rejected`: a second call (one fragment per instance, as MR-7); a fragment whose instance or role is not this Module's Provider; a fragment content with no `Requested`; any `rejected` entry of its own `coerce`; a primary root that is not its device's (UR-6); an attached link that is not a `StreamOut` on one of the fragment's receive ports `rx<k>` for `k` below the receive stream count (RM-29), or one whose policy is `Block` (a device cannot wait for a consumer; it overflows); several links on one port are allowed, each receiving every block of that port's stream (UR-17).
  For each stream whose channel set is not empty, it configures that stream: its set is RM-30's (`radio.<d>.c<n>.stream`), its rate is `radio.<d>.s<k>.sample_rate_hz`, and each channel takes its own numbered frequency, gain and antenna. A stream with no channel declares no SampleClock and configures nothing. It declares a SampleClock for each non-empty stream — `<id>/rx/s<k>` and `<id>/tx/s<k>` — on the primary root with `root_ticks_per_tick = N`. It keeps `ctx`'s handles and returns `PrepareReport { effective: RM-30's numbered keys with the applied values, coercions: its coerce's, warnings: [] }` (MA-12). A configure the device fails with its loss is published (UR-29) before `prepare` refuses with the error's own text.

  **Configuring a stream** — in `prepare`, when a `start_rx` begins a segment (UR-25), after a configuration that did not succeed (UR-13), and, on a transmit stream, for a burst whose generation the device does not hold (UR-26), and nowhere else — applies the stream's current configuration to every channel of its set, untimed, and reads it back: at `prepare`, at a `start_rx` and after a failure the full configuration (rate, frequency, gain, antenna), and for a transmit burst its frequency, gain and antenna without a rate, the rate of a transmit stream's clock being fixed while it runs; for every configuration the read-back rate must be exactly the claimed one, with `N = round(mcr / rate)` (`mcr` the profile's master clock, UR-9) one of the DDC's decimations (design 33 D5) and `mcr / N` computed in `f64` equal to `rate` — MockRadio's rule (`ezsdr_radio::x3xx::decimation`) —, so that every advertised rate is accepted; the SampleClock's ratio is `N / 1`, exact whatever the `f64` rate's last bit; else the configuration is refused (`"UR-12: the device applied <rate> S/s for the claimed <claim>"`, or `"UR-12: <rate> S/s is no decimation of 200000000 Hz"`); read-back frequencies and gains are recorded beside the claims in the `applied` section, with their difference, and do not refuse; a claimed antenna's read-back is recorded and compared, a disagreement being refused (`"UR-12: the device reads back <read> for the claimed <antenna> antenna of radio.<d>.c<n>.antenna`).

  *Test:* `ur_12_prepare_refusals` (a Simulation class refused `Unsupported`; a second call and a `Block` link each refused with their message; `radio.rx.c2.frequency_hz` is rejected with no device apply), `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_declares_its_clock_and_opens_its_channels`, `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_applies_each_channel_s_own_settings`, `ur_12_a_layout_s_stream_zero_with_no_channel_declares_no_clock`, `ur_12_a_layout_declares_and_starts_every_stream`, `ur_12_a_layout_a_rate_the_device_does_not_apply_is_refused` and `ur_12_a_layout_an_antenna_read_back_that_differs_is_refused` (the layout's stream 0: its set, its clock on `usrp/rx/s0`, its per-channel settings, its empty set, a later stream's declaration and start, and the two read-back refusals), `ur_12_a_tx_only_layout_declares_no_receive_clock` (a layout with no receive front end registers no receive SampleClock and configures no receive channel), `ur_12_the_applied_values_are_recorded` (a frequency's claimed and read-back values and a gain coerced to 10.5), `ur_12_a_rate_the_device_does_not_apply_is_refused` (the reason string) and `ur_12_every_advertised_rate_is_accepted` (all 639 decimation rates, `200e6 / 257`, `19.5e6` and `200e6 / 513` refused, the fake's clip checked, `200e6 / 3` prepared and captured); none named for the
`Requested`-parse and other-instance refusals, the not-a-`StreamOut`-link refusal and the `source`-not-a-node refusal. The loopback's phase after a receive frequency applied untimed at connect is a bench observation that decides no value, `hw_ma1_phase_after_a_pre_start_tune`, rehearsed on the fake by `the_ma1_phase_is_read_against_the_waveform_and_a_weak_or_unsteady_loop_is_inconclusive` (the phase read against the waveform, a weak or unsteady loop inconclusive).
  `ur_29_a_lost_apply_in_prepare_is_published_and_refused_with_the_devices_text` and `ur_12_a_refused_apply_in_prepare_that_is_not_a_loss_publishes_nothing` (a configure's loss is published before `prepare` refuses; a refusal that is not a loss publishes nothing).
- **UR-13** `arm()` records the arm instant `A = now(root)` and `S = A + startup_latency_ns`; when `clock_source` is not `internal`, refuses with `Rejected` a reference that `ref_locked` reports unlocked (`"UR-13: the <clock_source> reference is not locked"`); opens the streamer of each receive stream whose channel set is not empty and whose port has a link, and the streamer of each transmit stream whose set is not empty (RM-29); after each direction's first streamer opens, compares the rates the device reports at that streamer's first channel with the profile's grid, as a set (`ezsdr_radio::device::rate_set_matches`), refusing a mismatch or a read that fails with `UR-13:` (a lost read is published first, UR-29, and holds no outcome); a failing `ref_locked` read refuses `UR-13:` too; after a successful open whose comparison refuses, its caller closes the streamer at once (UR-36), but never closes a lost device or one whose read found it lost; compares no direction whose streamer it did not open (the `device` section records which were, UR-30); the outcome is the direction's, so every later open of it re-enforces it, a pass or a refusal; and registers each transmit stream's SampleClock, when declared, at the first instant at or after `A` on its lattice (RM-25, TM-13e). It radiates no experiment data (MA-13); UR-22's all-zero T3 open flush is not an experiment buffer.
  *Test:* `ur_13_arm_cases` (an unlocked external reference refused at `arm` with that reason, and a locked one running), `ur_13_arm_compares_the_rate_set_after_the_streamer_opens`, `ur_13_arm_refuses_a_rate_set_the_description_does_not_have`, `ur_13_arm_refuses_a_pre_chain_rate_set_after_the_open`, `ur_13_a_direction_the_arm_never_opened_is_not_compared` and `ur_13_arm_takes_the_rate_set_in_any_order` (the comparison's place, its refusal and its record), `ur_13_a_layout_s_transmit_stream_zero_opens_its_channel_list` and `ur_13_a_refusal_at_arm_that_is_not_a_loss_is_unchanged`, `ur_13_an_open_whose_rate_comparison_refuses_closes_the_streamer`, `ur_13_a_reopen_whose_comparison_refuses_closes_the_confirmed_streamer` and `ur_13_a_rate_read_that_fails_refuses` (a failing read is a refusal, never "no rates"), `ur_13_arm_radiates_nothing` (the transmit streamer opens; `arm` sends only the T3 flush and consumes its acknowledgement, with no experiment buffer) and `ur_15_every_clock_starts_on_its_lattice`; none named for the transmit clock's origin being the first lattice instant at or after `A`.
- **UR-14** The **control thread** `uhd-control`, started by `start`, runs until `stop` a loop that every millisecond drains the `ActionReceiver` with MA-14b's loop, **booking** each Action as UR-21, UR-25 and UR-26 say before taking the next — a refusal at booking names the Action's `Dispatched.id` as RM-22's `action`, and one of something booked earlier, refused as it applies, names none, as in MockRadio —: it validates, decides and records what the Action asks and hands device work to its owner — a burst to uhd-tx, a `start_rx` start to uhd-rx — and never waits for a device instant. In the same loop, every 500 ms it runs UR-27's reference check and reads the device's time (UR-29). The millisecond (POLL) and the 500 ms (UR-27, UR-29) are counts of the device's host clock (UR-2, UR-33), so on the manual clock a busy Provider thread stops them with the rest of the device's time. The Module's **delivery allowance** is §3's (MA-10) on that clock too: the poll period plus the time to book the Actions ahead in the queue; its **device lead**, the lead it needs from receipt, is §3's (UR-21).
  *Test:* `ur_14_each_action_is_booked_before_the_next_recv` (an `ActionReceiver` that notes each `recv` made before the previous Action's refusal is on record sees none); none named for the rest.
- **UR-15** `start(at)` takes `T0 = at` converted to the root and rounded up (the coordinator always passes `Some`). A `T0` before `S` emits `radio.LATE_COMMAND { key: null, requested: T0, applied: S }` and returns `Rejected` (`"UR-15: the start at <T0> precedes the device's start-up at <S>; the profile needs ezsdr.time.start_lead_ns ≥ 2000000000"`), and no stream starts (MR-11's form). Otherwise, when a receive stream's streamer is open, that stream's first segment begins at T0 — on the lattice, KC-15 —: it issues a timed continuous start at T0 (`rx_start(k, T0)`), so that the first sample is tick 0 at T0, and uhd-rx registers the stream's SampleClock with origin T0 at its first block (RM-25, UR-17); then it starts one uhd-rx and one uhd-tx per declared stream — `uhd-rx-s<k>` and `uhd-tx-s<k>` —, and uhd-control: a stream's owner starts whether or not that stream has channels (an owner without a streamer idles). `start` returns without waiting for T0.
  *Test:* `ur_15_start_cases` (an early T0 refused with one `LATE_COMMAND` and a reason beginning `"UR-15: the start at"`, and the first receive block at index 0 otherwise) and `ur_15_every_clock_starts_on_its_lattice`; a loss an owner published stops the next stream's start before its `rx_start`, by `ur_15_a_loss_published_by_an_owner_stops_the_next_stream_s_start`; none named for the rest of the reason and the event's payload.
- **UR-16** Every thread body runs under `catch_unwind`; a panic is recorded in the `rejected` section and reported as `DEVICE_LOST` with the message `"a Provider thread panicked"` (MA-9a), whichever thread it was — uhd-rx, uhd-tx or uhd-control — and whether or not `stop` has begun. Under every Policy the Provider's streams are then ended, and no new device call is initiated once their owners see the loss; work already admitted completes, samples already handed to the device are not recalled, and the Provider does not promise that the radio is silent at once. Under the default Policy the Run ends; under a Policy that maps `DEVICE_LOST` to `continue` it goes on without a working Provider, and, when uhd-control has died, the next Action submitted to the radio waits KC-21a's budget and fails the Run (after a uhd-rx or uhd-tx panic uhd-control still drains and acknowledges Actions). A device lost this way is not closed by `cleanup` (UR-29): it and its streamers are kept until the same `args` are opened again in the process or the process exits.
  Every join the Provider makes is bounded: 1 s of real time per thread (the joining participant is idle during the clock wait; the join bound remains real time), so that its `stop` and `cleanup` together stay within RS-8a's 5 s step deadline on a healthy device; a thread that does not join in time is recorded in `rejected` under its name — `uhd-rx-s<k>`, `uhd-tx-s<k>` or `uhd-control` — and left detached, and then `cleanup` does **not** call `close_streams`, which closes every streamer of the device — it records the leak instead.
  Every receive streamer the Module opens (at `arm`, at an owner's configure, at a reopen) is stopped untimed once, right after its `rx_open` and before any `rx_start`, so that a timed start an earlier close left queued (UR-33) is cancelled before the streamer is used. A stop on open that fails refuses the open with `UR-16: the untimed stop on open: <reason>` — at `arm` in `rejected` as `UR-13: UR-16: …`, at an owner as the segment's `COMMAND_REJECTED` (UR-24, UR-25) with no start issued —, and the streamer is not closed; one that fails with `lost: true` is published as UR-29 says — the receive open at `arm` (its open or its stop) publishes the loss before the refusal is returned, so that `cleanup` closes nothing — and nothing follows. `cleanup` adds no stop. A receive stream's stop precedes the `close_streams` that follows it (`Provider::stop`, UR-17).
  *Test:* `ur_16_a_panicking_thread_is_a_lost_device` (a panicking uhd-rx becomes `DEVICE_LOST`, is recorded in `rejected` and the device is marked lost before any `close_streams`), `ur_16_a_panicking_thread_ends_a_two_stream_run_with_its_message` (a Run with both directions' streams ends `Stopped { policy { DEVICE_LOST } }`, once, with the rule's message, and `rejected` holds the thread and reason), `ur_16_a_wedged_thread_does_not_wedge_stop` (a wedged uhd-tx is recorded and the stop still returns) and `ur_16_cleanup_keeps_the_streamer_of_a_detached_thread` (no `close_streams`, the leak recorded); a panicking uhd-control, by `ur_16_a_uhd_control_panic_ends_the_run_with_a_lost_device` (`Stopped { policy { DEVICE_LOST } }` once, the panic in `rejected`), `ur_16_a_uhd_control_panic_while_a_submit_waits_ends_it_with_the_loss` (not at KC-21a's budget), `ur_16_a_uhd_control_panic_under_a_continuing_policy_still_stops_the_repeat` (no new transmit or receive call in the second 50 ms window after the loss) and, as units, `ur_16_a_uhd_control_panic_before_a_uhd_tx_panic_is_one_device_lost` (one `DEVICE_LOST`, one `mark_lost`) and `ur_16_a_uhd_control_panic_holding_the_line_lock_does_not_deadlock_device_lost`; none named for a panic of uhd-tx alone (recorded and `DEVICE_LOST`) and a panic inside the handler itself;
  the stop on open by `ur_16_an_open_stops_the_streamer_before_its_first_start_and_cancels_an_orphaned_start` (`rx_open`, `rx_stop`, then the next `rx_start`; silence through the orphan's origin), `ur_16_a_failed_stop_on_open_refuses_the_segment_and_starts_nothing`, `ur_29_a_loss_found_by_the_stop_on_open_is_published_once_and_nothing_follows`, `ur_29_a_loss_found_by_the_stop_on_open_at_arm_is_published_once_and_cleanup_closes_nothing`, `ur_15_a_failed_rx_owner_spawn_abandons_its_started_chain_before_cleanup` (a queued start closed by `cleanup` is stopped by the next open) and `ur_26_provider_stop_before_a_distant_t0_stops_before_it_closes` (the stop precedes `close_streams`, nothing joins late, nothing is delivered through the old origin); none named for a stop on open of a fresh streamer holding no start being harmless on `UhdDevice` (INFERRED until the 2026-11-15 bench).
    The join's wait is checked by `ur_16_join_waits_idle_on_the_manual_clock_so_a_thread_that_needs_the_clock_ends` (on the manual clock the joining participant is idle while it waits, so a thread that needs the clock to advance ends and is joined) and its 1 s bound by `ur_16_the_join_bound_cuts_in_real_time_while_the_manual_clock_is_held` (a held manual clock does not stretch it: the thread is recorded and left detached after 1 s of real time).

### Receive

- **UR-17** uhd-rx carries out the receive plan it shares with uhd-control (UR-25), reading it at every turn. It calls `rx_recv(k, block_len, 100 ms)` in a loop, asking for no more than up to a pending cut. It takes as the cut the later of the cut it was given and the first sample it has not delivered, discards samples at or after that cut, and stops the stream untimed once its samples reach it or it has been silent past it for one block plus §3's delivery allowance, recording the stop in `applied` with the cut and the instant it was issued, and ends the clock at the cut.
  Its source is `<id>/rx/s<k>` (RM-29). For the next segment it reopens the streamer unless the device record holds its channel set, configures the stream with the segment's configuration (UR-36), issues a timed start there once configured, and registers the clock at its first block (RM-25). A cut at or before the first sample not yet delivered stops the stream untimed and ends it at once, whether or not the segment's origin has passed: the stop cancels a timed start still queued, and none of its samples is delivered. A cut after that sample leaves the segment running to the cut. An owned stream that ends in the ordinary way stops, and so cancels a queued start, before its streamer is closed (UR-16, UR-26); a queued start a close left behind anyway — an uncertain cleanup, a failed owner spawn — is cancelled by the next open's stop (UR-16).
  Each receive stream's device state is its `RxChain`, kept under a chain lock beside the plan: `Off`, `Armed` (a timed start issued for an origin), `Streaming`, `Uncertain` (after a failed stop or a stream abandoned without its owner) or `Lost`, with the segment it runs and the owner's intent (a cut, an end now, a termination); only the chain's transitions change them, and `Lost` is terminal.
  A configuration UR-12 refuses at a `start_rx` halts its stream (RM-25), with `COMMAND_REJECTED` and UR-12's reason: uhd-rx books the start as refused itself, and then begins the plan's next segment, which may begin where the refused one would have.
  uhd-rx keeps, in its stream's phase while it is active, the first sample of its segment not yet delivered (even when the plan no longer has the segment it began), where a booking reads it, updated with the cut it reads for each block: a loss and `Provider::stop` are booked with it (RM-16's floor, `Item.delivered`), so under `abort` the plan cuts the running segment there, and uhd-rx ends it at once; once `Provider::stop(abort)` has begun, uhd-rx ends a segment still being delivered up to an earlier cut there too, as soon as it reads the plan; and the end takes that sample only for the segment it cuts, still running at the end. Once the device is lost it reads the plan again and ends the stream at the loss's cut, which the thread that found the loss booked under the plan's lock before any thread can find the device lost. When a segment ends, the timeline's items before the command that began it are pruned.
  A result's first tick `f` gives the index `k = (f − origin) / N`, rounded to the nearest sample off the lattice (`stats.rx_off_lattice`). A block after the expected index carries `GAP_BEFORE` and `lost` its jump, with the flags UR-18 and UR-19 left pending; one overlapping what was delivered is trimmed (`stats.rx_overlapping`) and dropped if empty; one before its segment's origin or the previous stream's untimed stop is dropped (`stats.rx_before_origin`). Its bytes are SC-4's planar `cf32` from a `HostPool` (HD-2), published as `SampleBlock::new_host` with `direction: rx` and `valid` full to every link of its port (SC-11); a publish not `Accepted` is counted (`stats.link_drops_seen`).
  A `LateCommand` at a segment's start is a missed origin: `radio.LATE_COMMAND { key: null, requested: origin, applied: T1 }`, a timed start at `T1`, the first lattice instant at or after now plus UR-9's `radio.timing.rx_start_lead_ns`, and a first block with `GAP_BEFORE` and `lost` its index, the origin unchanged (SC-13, RM-25); while a cut is pending it is no missed start, and the stream is stopped untimed.
  *Test:* `ur_17_blocks_carry_the_device_timestamps`, `ur_17_a_missed_start_restarts_with_a_gap`, `ur_17_an_overlap_trimmed_to_nothing_is_dropped`, `ur_17_a_request_ends_at_the_pending_cut`, `ur_17_a_cut_is_never_before_what_was_delivered`, `ur_25_a_stopped_stream_ends_at_its_cut_and_its_late_tail_stays_off_the_new_clock`, `ur_24_a_missed_start_after_a_loss_makes_no_restart_call`, `ur_26_a_receive_start_of_no_planned_segment`, `ur_25_a_refused_segment_is_booked_by_uhd_rx`, `ur_26_uhd_rx_follows_the_stop_it_reads`, `ur_17_an_ended_segment_prunes_the_items_before_it`, `ur_17_blocks_reach_every_link`, `ur_26_a_cut_at_a_queued_origin_stops_the_stream_at_once`, `ur_26_a_cut_after_a_queued_origin_keeps_the_stream`, `ur_26_a_cut_after_the_origin_of_a_silent_start_stops_at_once`, `ur_26_a_stop_and_provider_stop_in_one_turn_stop_a_queued_start_once`, `ur_26_a_start_after_a_cancelled_queued_start_delivers_at_its_own_origin`, `ur_25_a_stop_between_start_rx_and_its_origin_leaves_no_trace`, `ur_29_a_loss_found_before_the_stop_of_a_queued_start_makes_no_stop_call`, `ur_29_a_stop_of_a_queued_start_that_finds_the_loss_publishes_it_once`, `ur_29_a_loss_ends_the_stream_at_its_instant`, `ur_29_a_loss_found_after_uhd_rx_read_the_plan_ends_the_stream`, `ur_29_a_silent_stream_ends_at_a_cut_booked_during_its_wait`, `ur_29_a_loss_takes_effect_before_a_change_at_its_instant`, `ur_26_an_end_takes_delivered_only_from_the_segment_it_cuts`,
  the chain's states (`Off`, `Armed`, `Streaming`, `Uncertain`, `Lost`) and their transitions by `rx_chain_adopt_start_transitions`, `rx_chain_begin_transitions`, `rx_chain_restart_transitions`, `rx_chain_first_sample_transition`, `rx_chain_stop_transitions`, `rx_chain_finish_transitions`, `rx_chain_lose_is_terminal` and `rx_chain_abandon_unowned_records_cleanup_state`; `ur_19_a_layout_receive_report_names_its_stream` (a layout report's source is `usrp/rx/s0`) and `rm_26_the_timeline_against_uhd_rx` (which checks the reopen, the cuts and the recorded untimed stops); the per-block timestamps and flags by `ur_17_an_overlap_trimmed_to_nothing_is_dropped` and, after a report, `ur_19_reports_before_one_block_keep_each_kind`; the missed-start block's `GAP_BEFORE` and positive `lost` by `ur_17_a_missed_start_without_another_report_is_not_an_overflow` and `ur_17_a_missed_start_and_overrun_share_the_block_summary`; the block's planar `cf32` bytes by `ur_17_a_block_s_channels_are_planar_cf32` and the restart's lead from the decision's `now` by `ur_17_the_missed_start_lead_is_measured_from_the_decision_s_now` (both unit); none named for the reference every link shares, the index rounded to the nearest sample off the lattice, and `stats.rx_off_lattice`, `stats.rx_before_origin` and `stats.link_drops_seen`.
- **UR-18** An `Overflow { out_of_sequence: false }` is RM-17's overrun: the next block carries `GAP_BEFORE | RESTARTED` with `lost` its whole time jump. An `Overflow { out_of_sequence: true }` or a `BadPacket` is RM-18's sequence error: `GAP_BEFORE | SEQ_DISCONTINUITY` with `lost` the jump. The reports before one jump block produce RM-17's single `RX_OVERFLOW`; a missed-start-only gap, or a jump with no `RX_OVERFLOW` report, produces no `RX_OVERFLOW` event.
  *Test:* `ur_19_reports_before_one_block_keep_each_kind`, `ur_17_a_missed_start_without_another_report_is_not_an_overflow`, `ur_17_a_missed_start_and_overrun_share_the_block_summary`, `rm_26_the_timeline_against_uhd_rx`, `vh_8_a_reported_overrun_matches_the_block_jump` and `vh_8_a_reported_sequence_error_matches_the_block_jump`; the overrun's `cause`, its `lost` and the `OverflowRestart` gap they make by `rehearsal_b5_overflow` (unit) and the bench's `hw_b5_overflow`; none named for a `BadPacket` as the sequence error.
- **UR-19** An `Alignment` result means the device discarded samples to keep the channels aligned: UHD never delivers part of a channel set (§2), so the next block carries `GAP_BEFORE | ALIGNMENT` on every channel with `lost` its time jump (SC-13), and `radio.ALIGNMENT_ERROR { lost }` is emitted on the control path from the receive stream when that block arrives. A jump block retains each distinct report's flag. The `RX_OVERFLOW` reports before that block produce at most one event with its whole `lost` and overrun as `cause` if present, otherwise sequence; alignment remains `ALIGNMENT_ERROR`. A report consumed by a block without a jump produces no `RX_OVERFLOW` and no report flag, and is not carried to a later block.
  *Test:* `ur_19_reports_before_one_block_keep_each_kind`, `ur_18_a_report_on_a_gapless_block_is_consumed_without_an_event` and `ur_19_a_layout_receive_report_names_its_stream` (the layout's source).
- **UR-20** `radio.RX_OVERFLOW` is emitted on the hot path (`EventSink::emit`) from uhd-rx with RM-24's 9 bytes and the `EventHandle` resolved in `prepare` for `(<id>/rx/s<k>, radio.RX_OVERFLOW)` for each receive stream `k` (RM-29); every other event is emitted with `emit_control` from the thread that decided it, with RM-11's source, time and payload (RM-11).
  *Test:* `ur_19_reports_before_one_block_keep_each_kind` (the delivered payload is the byte array and `RxOverflowPayload::from_payload` reads it) and `ur_19_a_layout_receive_report_names_its_stream` (the handle the layout's `usrp/rx/s0` source).

### Transmit

- **UR-21** uhd-control refuses a `TxBurst` with `radio.COMMAND_REJECTED` when RM-13 refuses it, and, as this Module's own, when its `at` is not on its target stream's last registered transmit clock, or when the waveform's bytes are not an input of the Run or their length is not `size_bytes`. A booking whose region meets an accepted booking's is refused here at receipt (RM-15) with `UR-21: the burst overlaps an accepted booking`; one that starts before an accepted chain the hand-over horizon may already have handed is refused there too, with `UR-21: the burst starts before a booking that may already be handed` (RM-15). A start, end or switch boundary that does not fit the transmit clock is refused with `UR-21: the burst time overflows the transmit clock` (RM-13). An untimed burst is placed at the first free sample at or after its earliest instant (RM-15), never judged late at receipt or at dispatch, and is refused for overlap only when no sample is free; a timed `SendAsap` burst whose start meets only the Stop region moves past it with `TIME_ERROR { send_asap }`, and a timed `DropAndFlag` one is refused.
  Otherwise uhd-control decides the burst as RM-14 says, with §3's device lead, and hands a held burst to uhd-tx.
  When uhd-tx moves a held head at its dispatch (its first send is late there), the moved region is judged against every accepted booking except the head itself, its joined pieces included; a collision refuses the burst with `TIME_ERROR { outcome: refused }` and `COMMAND_REJECTED` naming its Action, and the refused head's region leaves the overlap rule while its end still counts for values (RM-21).
  An untimed head that uhd-tx moves at its dispatch is placed after its joined pieces (RM-15), with its own span removed first, no hand-over horizon and no `TIME_ERROR`.
  Before its first timed start-of-burst hand-over, uhd-tx repeats the device-lead decision against its current instant, as RM-14 says; an adjacent continuation of an already open device burst has no timed start and is decided by the joined-burst rule instead; a first send that waits for window space (UR-23) is decided again once its start is less than the device lead away, before it is handed.
  **Joined bursts.** A held burst whose start is the open device burst's next sample (`chain.continues_at`) joins it, and no late policy is applied at that sample. It is decided at its dispatch like any held burst (RM-14): a late `send_asap` moves it only if the move meets no accepted region, and a collision refuses it with `TIME_ERROR { outcome: refused }` and `COMMAND_REJECTED` naming its Action; a late `drop_and_flag` burst is dropped as RM-14 says. The join happens only while a Kernel burst is open (`join_open`); otherwise the burst starts on its own.
  *Test:* `ur_21_txburst_refusals` (a `size_bytes` that is not a multiple of `8 · channels`, a non-finite component and a booking whose region meets an accepted one's, each naming its Action's id), `ur_21_a_burst_time_that_does_not_fit_the_clock_is_refused_with_its_id_and_the_next_booking_is_served` and `ur_38_a_switch_at_the_end_of_the_range_is_refused_with_its_id_and_the_next_one_is_taken` (a start, a repeat, a target whose lead overflows and a switch at `i64::MAX`), `ur_21_a_burst_before_start_up_is_refused` (a start before the synchronisation end), `ur_21_late_policies_on_the_device_lead` (the receipt site's `Drop` and `SendAsap`), `ur_21_an_untimed_send_is_placed_after_its_delivery` (unit, controlled clock: a send stamped at its receipt plus the 5 ms lead, read 1 ms, 3 ms or 4 ms after receipt, is placed at the later of that instant and its ordinary start, with no `TIME_ERROR`), `ur_21_a_burst_inside_the_in_flight_window_is_refused` (a booking inside an accepted finite burst's span is refused at receipt, on time or late, for both late policies), `ur_21_a_send_asap_move_is_reported_once_across_handovers` (the later hand-over re-evaluates the boundary without a second event),
  `ur_21_s18_an_untimed_repeat_on_a_playing_repeat_is_refused_without_time_error` and `ur_21_s19_untimed_switch_takes_the_first_grid_boundary_after_its_receipt_window` (an untimed repeat on a playing repeat is refused as an overlap; an untimed switch takes its timed twin's boundary), `ur_21_s15_host_lateness_moves_an_untimed_burst_silently` (an untimed head moved at dispatch: no `TIME_ERROR`), `ur_21_s16_timed_send_asap_in_the_stop_region_moves_with_time_error` and `ur_21_s16_timed_drop_in_the_stop_region_is_refused` (the timed Stop-region rule), `ur_21_an_untimed_repeat_after_a_stop_starts_at_the_bound_or_its_ordinary_start` (unit, controlled clock: an untimed repeat received inside a Stop region starts at its bound, and one received after it at its ordinary start),
   `ur_21_s1_overlap_refused_at_receipt`, `ur_21_s5_b_handed_before_an_earlier_a_is_refused_at_receipt`, `ur_23_s3_dispatch_move_into_a_later_booking_is_refused`, `ur_23_s3_refused_move_frees_its_front`, `ur_21_s6_send_asap_moves_past_an_accepted_span`, `ur_21_s6_send_asap_past_a_later_booking_is_placed_after_it`, `ur_21_send_does_not_cross_the_in_flight_window`, `ur_21_tx_reads_in_flight_window_from_description`, `ur_21_a_new_burst_without_an_open_predecessor_is_on_time`, `ur_21_s2_a_finite_front_stops_at_its_end`, `ur_23_a_burst_at_a_closed_short_burst_s_end_is_late_at_dispatch` (the padded boundary), `ur_21_a_finite_burst_with_a_held_tail_keeps_its_front`, `ur_21_a_post_eob_restart_uses_the_safe_sample`, `ur_21_a_late_joined_burst_cannot_move_and_is_refused`,
    `ur_21_tx_front_is_per_stream`, `ur_21_a_target_after_a_completed_burst_is_on_time`, `ur_21_a_burst_before_its_clock_s_origin_is_late`, `ur_21_first_dispatch_rechecks_all_late_policies` (the moved start, its `requested_target` and `BurstOpen.late`), `ur_21_first_dispatch_preserves_an_on_time_start`, `ur_21_first_dispatch_refuses_a_moved_start_collision`, `ur_23_a_first_send_one_tick_inside_its_lead_follows_drop_and_flag`, `ur_23_a_first_send_at_its_lead_boundary_is_sent_by_drop_and_flag`, `ur_23_a_first_send_one_tick_inside_its_lead_follows_send_asap_and_flag`, `ur_23_a_first_send_at_its_lead_boundary_is_sent_by_send_asap_and_flag`; none named for the `at`-clock refusal or input-store and length refusals.
- **UR-22** Every payload-bearing `Device::tx_send` names the first sample's instant on the stream's SampleClock, including repeat chunks, continuations, held tails and closing sends. The only sample-bearing untimed send is T3's four-zero SOB/EOB flush, used on open or to recover an uncertain close. A first send of a device burst that stays open carries at least `MIN_OPEN_FIRST_SEND` = 32 samples per channel. A first send that ends its device burst (its waveform or a held burst's start) is closed by its own end-of-burst (UR-23): one of one to three samples is padded with zeros after its payload to `MIN_FIRST_SEND` = 4 and sent at once, and a closed first send of four or more needs no pad. A repeat's first send tiles its waveform to at least 32 payload samples. A continuation has no minimum; a one-sample continuation that closes the device burst is padded to two. A device burst closes only with an EOB send of at least two samples per channel, and a close with no payload sends two zeros. The Kernel burst record counts payload only. After a successful close, the next safe start is the closing send's exclusive end, padding included, plus `CLOSE_GAP` = 3 samples, or plus `g` = `max(3, ceil(15 µs × the stream's sample rate))` samples after a closed first send of at most 32 samples (the short-burst guard of UR-33; `ezsdr_radio::staging::closed_short_gap`, RM-15).
  A two-zero close of an open device burst at a `Stop` starts at the burst's cursor, or at its held tail, and fronts at that start plus five. The booking region of a closed first send is RM-15's: a send of at most 32 samples holds its exclusive end, padding included, plus `g`.
  VERIFIED (2026-11-10, both X300s, UHD only): one gap of at most about 10 µs after a short closed head at interpolation 60 to 400, one sample at 4 to 50, and gaps of 2 and 3 after any close at 100 and 200 MS/s. The three samples after any other close are the measured minimum at 200 MS/s (no margin) and leave a margin of one at 100 MS/s; below that one sample sufficed at interpolation 4 to 50, so there they are margin. The three-sample floor of `g` is margin at every rate on the transmit grid, where `g` is at least 15. INFERRED: every interpolation the sweep did not measure (the 15 µs margin covers it) and the Module's own close shapes.
  A short or failed EOB close does not advance the safe front: it is recorded in `rejected`, and the next timed burst on that stream follows the T3 flush path before its data is sent. Once recovery is pending, no payload is sent on that streamer until it is replaced and its T3 is confirmed (below); an ordinary TX `Stop` preserves that requirement, while terminal shutdown or device loss ends it. A send that fails or is short ends the Kernel burst, is recorded in `rejected`, and is not resumed; a sequence-loss report, and (for a burst that is not a repeat; a repeat resumes, below) an underflow report with no tick or with a tick not before the open device burst's first sample, abandons that burst, discards its held tail and records the recovery reason; an underflow report whose tick is before that first sample is an earlier device burst's: it is recorded and reported (UR-28) but abandons nothing.
  A `TimeError` report whose tick is not before the open device burst's first sample abandons that burst the same way, with the reason `UR-22: the device dropped the burst late; burst not resumed`, unless it is a repeat's (below); a `TimeError` with no tick abandons nothing, and neither does one before that first sample (an earlier device burst's). An abandon retires no correlation (UR-28). A still-open Kernel burst ends with `BurstTracker::stop`; future held bursts keep their reservations and undergo UR-21 at dispatch. Before sending another continuation, uhd-tx abandons a timeline whose next sample is already before its current instant, even if its asynchronous report has not arrived (a repeat resumes instead, below). Between buffers and while idle, uhd-tx reads asynchronous reports (`tx_async` with a zero timeout; UR-28).
