  **A repeat resumes.** An underflow report or a `TimeError` report that reaches the open device burst of a repeat past its first send, and uhd-tx's own finding that such a repeat's timeline has expired, do not abandon it. uhd-tx closes the device burst at the handed cursor `H` with the two-zero close of the abandon, keeps the booking open and paused there (the device chain, not the booking, says whether a device burst is open: none is while the booking is paused), and sends its next device burst, a timed start of burst tiled from phase 0 of the waveform with at least 32 samples, at `R`: the first point of the booking's lattice `origin + j·period` at or after the latest of `busy + m`, the decision instant plus the device lead and the device front. `busy` is the Module's own bound, per stream and across owners, on when the device finishes all it was handed: after each payload, held-tail and close send the device took whole, `busy ← max(busy, the instant the send returned, the send's first sample) + the send's samples` (the T3 flush is not counted: `R` is taken before it runs); `m` is its own constant of 10 ms (INFERRED until bench cells C4 and C7 measure it). The samples from `H` to `R` are not transmitted and are not sent again. Each resume writes one `timing` row `{ what: "tx_resume", stream, cause: "late_at_device" | "underflow" | "expired", from: H, resumes_at: R, at }` and no `rejected` note.
  The part's first block carries `START_OF_BURST`, so each part is a burst record of its own (SC-24): the one before ends `discontinuity` and the part's `target` is `R`. The event that tells the drop (UR-28) carries `resumes_at: R`; when the expired check resumed first, the report that comes later carries that plan, once.
  A part ends the repeat by the abandon above when a late report reaches it (nothing of it played) or an underflow reaches it after it played less than the stall part of the gap before it, `(busy + m) − H` at that resume (a part that played exactly that resumes again); so do a sequence-loss report (also while the repeat waits to resume), a failed or short send, a failed reopen, a lost device, and an `R` or a switch boundary that does not fit the clock. A burst that is not a repeat is never resumed. A report of a dropped device burst read after its part was sent reaches the part's correlation only if its tick is not before the part's first sample, which is its own start of burst's. A report read while a repeat waits to resume (an underflow or a late report) applies the stall rule only when it reaches the part whose drop made that pause (the pause remembers that device burst's first instant), not a report of another repeat's part.
  **A resume point that is missed moves.** uhd-tx judges `R` as any device burst's start (RM-14): an `R` less than the device lead past its decision instant (or before the device front, which a flush can move) is missed, and `R` moves to the first point of the lattice that is on time, never through UR-21's dispatch, which would move the repeat off its lattice; the part then starts there. A part that starts later than planned, by a missed `R` or by a switch (below), is told once, when it is sent: `TIME_ERROR { send_asap }` for its Kernel burst with `late_by_ns` the distance from the planned start (the plan the drop's event announced, or the switch's own boundary), however many times `R` moved. The stall part of the gap is unchanged by a move: the wait to a later boundary is not counted by the stutter rule.
  **A switch across the gap** (RM-37, UR-38) is neither cancelled nor refused. While the booking is paused, a held switch whose boundary is before `R + 32` (32 is a device burst's shortest first send) is settled against `R` at each turn: a boundary at or before `R` replaces the repeat at `R`, the switch's waveform being the booking that resumes (its lattice, its `switches` boundary and the part's start all `R`); a boundary within 32 samples after `R` moves to the first point of the replaced lattice at or after `R + 32`, where the repeat's part joins it as it does any switch; a later boundary is kept. A resume point that moves runs the rule again, and the switch is told late once, from the boundary it had at receipt. A switch whose boundary send was handed before the drop is applied at its boundary, and the repeat that resumes is the switch's. Between a switch's join and its first send (the device burst open, the switch held at the burst's cursor or open with nothing sent) a report or an expired timeline resumes the repeat the same way: the switch becomes the paused booking, its lattice taken from its own boundary (the replaced repeat's is no longer kept). A halt before the part is sent cancels the switch and ends the repeat's record.
  On TX streamer open, or to recover an uncertain EOB close, T3 sends an untimed four-zero SOB/EOB burst. The opener or recovering owner reads its per-channel BurstAck, or waits to the single 100 ms device-host-clock deadline and records a missing acknowledgement as `rejected { call: "tx_flush", stream, error }`; a read BurstAck is recorded in `async` with `tx_flush: true`. At the open, a missing acknowledgement does not itself stop the stream, while device loss and `Provider::stop` do; the recovery's T3 is confirmed as below. The flush precedes experiment data on that path. Untimed `prepare` configuration is unchanged. This flush clears stale DUC header state. An acknowledged flush, and a two-zero close that abandons an open burst, are closed short bursts: each fronts at its exclusive end, padding included, plus `g` (UR-33 gives the flush's end from its BurstAck); the abandon's end is its cursor plus two. The abandon's burst may be a closed short one on the device (its accepted samples and two zeros), so it keeps `g` and not three (INFERRED: the sweep measured standalone closed heads, not a partial first send).
  **A recovery's T3 is confirmed** by one attempt: its send took all four samples, and every channel position has a `BurstAck` whose tick is at or after the attempt's floor, the later of the device time read before its send and the old burst's padded end plus two ticks (the stream's previous front less `CLOSE_GAP`, on the stream's clock; no such term without a previous front). A burst's own acknowledgement is stamped one tick after its padded end, a T3 queued behind it at least four samples after. A time read that fails is retried every millisecond, until it succeeds, a `Provider::stop` or a device loss ends the loop, or it has failed for 2 s; the attempt's 100 ms starts after the read that succeeded. A read that has failed for 2 s ends the reopen at once, with no send and the streamer closed.
  An attempt whose send is not whole ends at once, and any other is unconfirmed at its 100 ms deadline. An unconfirmed attempt records `rejected { call: "tx_flush", stream, error, attempt }` and emits no event; the streamer is then closed and a fresh one opened, up to three attempts in all, and the first confirmed attempt ends the recovery, its largest tick giving the front as for an acknowledged flush. After the third unconfirmed attempt, or after 2 s of failing time reads (the cause `flush time read failed: …`), the streamer is closed and the reopen fails (UR-25): the stream's clock ends, each held burst is cancelled with its own `COMMAND_REJECTED` (`tx_burst`), and one `COMMAND_REJECTED` of kind `tx_reopen` names the failure, whatever the number of attempts. Its later bursts are refused with `UR-25: its transmit clock ended before the burst could start`.
  No payload is sent while the attempts last. The owner's turn waits for them, up to about 3 × (the close + the device open + 100 ms + one time read), plus up to 2 s of failing time reads before each attempt's send, and a held burst whose target passes meanwhile is judged by its late policy at its next dispatch (RM-14). `Provider::stop` and a device loss end the loop before the close, before the open's device call, between the open and the T3 send, before each time read of an attempt and again before its send, and inside an attempt; a `Provider::stop` that arrives inside the open call is met after it, with no T3 sent. The report gate stays held across the attempts, so no shutdown reader takes a report of the recovery, and a report an attempt stashed is kept for the owner (UR-28). A repeat switch pending across the attempts stays pending until the owner's next turn after the Kernel's clock follows the device's, and a failed recovery cancels it. At `arm` and in an owner's mid-run open the flush is one attempt (above).

- **UR-23** uhd-tx carries out the transmit plan it shares with uhd-control (UR-25), reading the cut at each of its turns: its stream's one clock is registered at `arm` (UR-13), and it holds the bursts booked on it. Held bursts are transmitted in target order (RM-15). A burst ends at the end of its waveform (without `repeat`), at the start of the next held burst (the current one is sent up to the sample before the next start), at a `Stop` for `<id>/tx/s<k>` or `<id>`, or at the Provider's `stop`. A loss or `Provider::stop` at or before the clock's origin cuts its segment at the origin, and the booking that cuts it ends the clock there (RM-25).
  Whether a device burst is open is the device chain's, not the open booking's: a repeat paused to resume (UR-22) is a booking past its first send with none open, and a `Stop` then sends nothing.
  **Device bursts.** Where the next burst starts at the current one's next sample — held at that start as a joined burst (RM-15) — the device burst continues: the current one's last buffer goes without end-of-burst and the next one's first without start-of-burst, with its time spec required by UR-22. The two Kernel bursts keep their own `START_OF_BURST`/`END_OF_BURST` blocks and records, and one `BURST_ACK` answers both. A current closed short device burst (at most 32 samples, UR-22) is already closed: no burst continues it, and the rule below decides a burst booked at its end.
  A next burst that starts later is a new device burst: the current one's last buffer carries end-of-burst — or, when that buffer already went out, UR-22's no-payload two-zero close does — so no timed start-of-burst falls inside an un-ended burst; the next starts with start-of-burst and its time spec. A Kernel burst's end is not at once the device burst's: while a burst could still be booked at its next sample, its last sample is held back from its last buffer, so that a burst booked there after that buffer went out still continues it; the held sample is sent with end-of-burst at the owner's first step after `J(e) = instant(e) − (device lead + delivery allowance)` at which no booking can still continue the device burst at `e` (RM-16), before the device runs dry; a booking that continues it there, received by `J(e)`, is sent without end-of-burst ahead of its first buffer.
  A last buffer sent when its end is already the device lead or less away carries end-of-burst itself, as does a one-sample burst with no reserved continuation. When a continuation is reserved at the next sample, the payload goes without end-of-burst so the continuation can join; if that continuation is cancelled before hand-over, UR-22's no-payload close ends the device burst. A first send of at most 32 samples that closes its device burst is not held back. A Kernel burst that starts before a closed short device burst's front (its end, padding included, plus `g` samples) is decided by its own late policy against that front, and does not continue it.
  A device burst left open for a continuation that does not come (the next burst stopped, cancelled or refused first), including a reserved continuation after a finite Kernel burst, are closed under UR-22; one whose send failed or came up short is abandoned there too. The abandon of a finite burst ends the burst's chain in the record in the same section (RM-15): a booking at its end is refused at receipt with `UR-21: the burst overlaps an accepted booking`, and a burst already held behind it is sent as a new device burst, not as its continuation; a finite abandon does not rewrite what a `Stop`, `Provider::stop` or a device loss already ended in the record. A repeat stopped in the middle of its waveform with nothing held is closed under the same rule, after the record's last payload sample.
  uhd-tx hands only payload samples before the first SampleClock sample at or after `now + radio.timing.tx_in_flight_ns`; it rounds the exclusive cursor up on that stream's sample lattice and does not hand a chunk past it. On `Stop`, the cursor is the amount already handed, no later than `s + W`; the EOB's zero padding is the stated exception. The record's end is the last payload sample actually handed over (RM-16).
  uhd-tx calls `Device::raise_thread_priority` once, as the first statement of its thread (`UhdDevice`: `uhd_set_thread_priority(0.5, true)`), and neither uhd-rx nor uhd-control calls it. Any `Err` is recorded as a `rejected` row `{ call: "set_thread_priority", stream, error }`, the stream runs on, and the Provider does not treat it as a loss of the device; a success is counted in `stats.tx_realtime`.
  uhd-tx hands a buffer only when every sample that the other limits allow lies inside the window: a TX buffer cap, a one-shot waveform's end, an untiled repeat's original-waveform wrap, or the next held burst's start. When a repeat shorter than the TX buffer cap is tiled to provide a full buffer at every original phase, a send may cross original-waveform wraps; phase and wrap accounting still use the original waveform length. It waits otherwise, handing the device nothing and leaving the held last sample untouched. A device burst's first timed buffer waits for 32 samples of window space (UR-22), or for its whole length when that is shorter, then takes what fits; a later buffer waits until the window holds every sample it may take.

### Parameter updates

- **UR-24** Withdrawn (note 47 stage 2): the timed release of `hardware_timed` updates — the held list, release window, queue depth and floor, the DDC debt and drain, and the restore of a receive frequency.

- **UR-25** Each stream's planned state is one `ezsdr_radio::timeline` line, shared by uhd-control, uhd-rx and uhd-tx under one lock that no thread holds across a device call, and booked by whichever thread learns an event: uhd-control the `Stop`s and `start_rx`s, uhd-rx a configuration its device refuses (UR-17), the thread that finds the device lost the loss (UR-29), and `Provider::stop` the streams' end (UR-26). A line holds a stream's cuts and starts; the configuration a start begins with is the snapshot `start_rx` took at its booking (UR-26).
  uhd-control books a `Stop` and a `start_rx` when it takes the Action (UR-14), its instant taken under that lock: it converts `at` (one it cannot convert is refused, never taken as absent), and `e` is `at`, or the booking when absent or past, and for a receive command with a receive link (RM-21) never before T0.
   It takes the stream's cuts and origins from `ezsdr_radio::timeline` with this Module's **ready instant**: the booking and, for a receive segment after a cut, at least that cut plus one receive call at the old rate (the longer of `block_len` and that stream's `rx_packet_samples(k)`) and §3's delivery allowance. A `Stop` cuts at its booking, `Provider::stop` at its instant. The owner configures a segment inside its start lead, after the booking, and `rx_start` goes out at the planned origin; when the configure ends with less than `min_timed_command_lead_ns` before it the owner emits `radio.START_LEAD_SHORT` first (RM-25, `timeline::lead_left`), and when it ends at or after the origin the device's missed start follows (UR-17).

  **Ownership.** Each stream's holder is `Free` or `Owner`, and its report read is a separate `reading` flag; only `Streams`' transitions change them, and a `Claim` returned by `claim_owner` holds the stream until `give_back`. uhd-tx's report read in flight is the stream's `reading` flag, which `begin_read` sets only on a `Free` stream and `end_read` clears; the shutdown's read is `begin_shutdown_read`, which takes no holder check, so that a uhd-control the join bound left detached (UR-16) cannot hold it. An owner that finds a holder other than `Free` makes no device call. uhd-tx sets `reading` for its `reports` call — the report reads and the sends a report's end of burst makes — and clears it after.

  A stream node (`<id>/rx/s<k>`, `<id>/tx/s<k>`) is a target: a `Stop` of `<id>` stops every stream and one of a stream node that stream, and `<id>/rx` and `<id>/tx` are no targets (RM-12, D14). A configuration UR-12 refuses at a `start_rx` is a refusal at its instant, which halts its stream (RM-25), with `COMMAND_REJECTED` and UR-12's reason. uhd-rx carries the receive plan out as UR-17 says, uhd-tx the transmit plan as UR-23 says; the plan has no segment after `Provider::stop`'s end; an owner that read its plan before that end may still issue the start it read (UR-26's window).
   *Test:* `rm_26_the_timeline_against_uhd_rx`, `ur_25_a_configure_that_leaves_less_than_the_declared_lead_reports_it_and_starts_at_the_origin`, `ur_25_a_configure_that_ends_after_the_origin_is_a_missed_start_reported_once`, `ur_25_a_short_lead_and_a_missed_start_are_each_reported_once`, `ur_25_a_booking_the_line_cannot_represent_is_refused_with_the_start_s_id`, `ur_25_a_receive_session_follows_its_recorded_plan`, `ur_25_tx_channels_from_zero_transmits_the_next_burst`, `ur_25_a_refused_segment_is_booked_by_uhd_rx`, `ur_29_an_owner_does_not_configure_a_segment_a_stop_dropped_from_the_plan`, `ur_25_an_end_booked_after_the_first_read_cancels_the_held_burst_at_the_origin`, `ur_26_no_segment_begins_once_provider_stop_has_begun`, `rm_26_a_pruned_line_plans_as_one_that_keeps_every_item`, `ur_26_d14_a_layout_instance_names_its_streams`, `ur_26_a_device_stop_stops_every_layout_stream`, `ur_26_d14_the_old_addresses_and_foreign_nodes_are_refused` and `ur_26_d14_a_channel_less_stream_keeps_the_mock_s_treatment`;
   the lock rule by `ur_25_the_line_lock_is_free_inside_every_receive_device_call`, `ur_25_the_line_lock_is_free_inside_every_transmit_device_call`, `ur_25_an_open_is_recorded_only_after_the_device_returns` and `ur_25_a_device_call_passes_while_another_thread_holds_the_line`;
   the holder and the read by `ur_25_a_claim_owner_takes_only_a_free_stream`, `ur_25_a_begin_read_needs_a_free_stream_and_no_read`, `ur_25_a_begin_shutdown_read_ignores_the_holder`, `ur_25_an_end_read_without_a_read_panics_in_the_test_build`, `ur_25_a_claim_dropped_without_give_back_panics_in_the_test_build`, `ur_25_a_give_back_of_another_holder_panics_in_the_test_build_and_changes_nothing`, `ur_25_a_detached_control_does_not_hold_the_shutdown_s_read`, `ur_25_a_refused_receive_owner_books_and_gives_back_in_one_line_section`, `ur_34_a_transmit_stop_books_the_wait_exit_and_queues_the_owner_stop` and `ur_34_a_rate_read_waiting_for_device_gate_does_not_call_after_loss`.

- **UR-36** Each stream keeps one record of what its device holds (UR-13), as fields of its own state, written under the stream's line lock by whichever thread made the device call and read there by any other: the channel set its streamer is open on, the streamer's **open generation**, and the command that began the last configuration the device was given, `seq` (for a transmit stream, the value generation the device was last configured with, set by `arm` and cleared by a failed configure or a loss, UR-26).
  The generation advances on every successful open of the stream, whoever made it, so that a correlation against the replaced streamer's asynchronous reports is told from the new one's (UR-28).
  An open that fails, or a rate comparison that refuses, leaves no set and no configuration known; when the open succeeded but its rate comparison refused, the caller closes the streamer before recording no set. A lost device is never closed. A successful open leaves its set and no configuration known.
  A configure whose every channel the device took, and whose read-backs UR-12 accepts, records its segment's command as the configuration the device was given, or no configuration where that segment's command is none, the stream's first; a configure that fails leaves no configuration known, its set unmoved.
  Pruning changes no part of the record. The record also holds the stream's pending repeat switch and its playing repeat, which is the switch itself from the switch's receipt (UR-38), under the same lock.
   *Test:* `ur_13_a_failed_open_by_the_owner_is_opened_again`, `ur_13_a_failed_configure_is_configured_again_without_reopen`, `ur_13_a_partly_applied_set_is_configured_whole_without_reopen`, `ur_36_every_restart_is_configured_with_the_connect_values`, `ur_36_the_owner_step_follows_the_record`, `ur_25_a_refused_check_at_arm_leaves_the_set_unknown`, `ur_13_arm_refuses_a_pre_chain_rate_set_after_the_open`,
   `ur_34_a_rate_refusal_leaves_the_stream_record_unknown`, `uhd_close_drops_its_map_reference_but_an_in_flight_arc_keeps_the_streamer`, `uhd_stream_close_and_open_compete_under_the_control_mutex` and `ur_28_a_streamer_the_record_holds_keeps_its_correlations`.
- **UR-37** The owner of a planned receive segment whose `start_rx` the device refused (UR-25) makes no device call for it: it opens no streamer, configures nothing and starts nothing. A refused receive start is left out of its stream's plan, so its owner reaches no such segment (UR-17).
  *Test:* `ur_25_a_refused_segment_is_booked_by_uhd_rx`, `ur_25_a_refused_start_of_a_later_stream_halts_that_stream_alone` and `ur_25_a_successful_start_clears_a_later_stream_s_halt`.
- **UR-38** uhd-control books a repeat switch (RM-37: a `TxBurst` with `repeat: true`, late policy `send_asap_and_flag` and the switch mark, UR-21) at its receipt. It is refused with its id, as `COMMAND_REJECTED`, when the stream has no repeat playing or a switch is pending (a repeat paused to resume, UR-22, still plays, so a switch is accepted across its gap) (one stays pending until the boundary send settles it, so also after its boundary has passed unsent), and with `UR-38: the repeat the switch replaces has not started on the device` when uhd-tx has issued none of that repeat (a switch's new repeat starts with the first issue of its own waveform; KL-41). Otherwise its boundary is `repeat_boundary` of the playing repeat's origin and period, at or after the later of the burst's `at` and the receipt's sample plus the window and the device lead, the record holds it pending, and it is the stream's playing repeat from then on.
  The boundary is not judged late at receipt and nothing moves it but a gap of the repeat it replaces (UR-22): uhd-tx reads every receipt waiting for it before it hands anything further, so it holds the new waveform at the boundary before its cursor reaches it, and continues the device burst into it there.
  The pending switch is applied once uhd-tx has sent the sample at its boundary (the first send of the switch's own waveform, the *boundary send*, whole or short with at least one sample taken) and device time reaches the boundary. A Stop whose bound is at or before that boundary (RM-16) and an F8 recovery that ends the repeat cancel it; any other Stop and `Provider::stop` settle it by the boundary send (sent: applied, once device time reaches the boundary or the Manifest is written, unless a loss at a sample at or before the boundary comes first; not issued or none taken: cancelled; ended in an exception: uncertain, also when such a loss comes), and a loss settles it by device time at the loss (applied when the boundary send was taken and the sample at the boundary was played; uncertain when the send ended in an exception; otherwise cancelled). A Stop or a loss that comes while the boundary send is in flight leaves the switch pending until its result, unless its bound is at or before the boundary, which cancels the switch at once. The result then settles it, except that a whole result under a Stop that came before device time reached the boundary keeps it pending until then.
  A boundary send that has no result when the Manifest is written (its owner panicked, or was left detached, UR-16) settles the switch as a send that ended in an exception: uncertain; a sent switch that a Stop left pending before device time reached the boundary is applied there. `Provider::stop` books the Run's end on every transmit stream before it shuts the transmit threads down (UR-26), so it decides the switch there too. Its `switches` row (UR-30) holds the action, the predecessor, the receipt's instant, the old and new periods, the boundary (`boundary`) and the outcome, which the record writes when it settles the switch: `boundary` is the one the switch settled at, which is the boundary chosen at receipt unless a gap of the repeat it replaces moved it (UR-22); the new waveform's burst record carries the switch's own `at` as `requested_target` and the move as `send_asap` (RM-14).
  An abandon of the repeat at a handed cursor at or before the boundary cancels it too (RM-37), and its new waveform, read or unread, is never sent. An abandon past the boundary halts at device time, not at its cursor: a whole boundary send is applied once device time reaches the boundary, and a loss at a sample at or before the boundary before then cancels it. An F8 recovery cancels a switch a Stop is still holding.
  *Test:* `ur_21_s8_switch_installed_before_the_boundary_sample`, `o1_uhd_t4b_an_owner_that_has_not_read_a_switch_never_hands_past_its_boundary`, `ur_21_s4_a_switch_whose_repeat_the_host_dispatched_late_is_refused_at_receipt`, `o1_uhd_t8_a_switch_whose_repeat_the_host_has_not_issued_is_refused_at_receipt`, `e2a_84_a_second_switch_on_a_switch_the_host_has_not_issued_is_refused`, `e2a_b1_a_switch_behind_an_unstarted_finite_burst_is_refused`, `e2a_f1_a_sequence_error_cancels_a_switch_whose_payload_is_unread`, `e2a_f1_a_failed_send_with_a_switch_received_in_flight_cancels_it`, `e2a_s11_a_stop_after_a_switch_whose_boundary_is_before_the_bound_cancels_it_and_its_payload`, `e1_q4b_a_switch_read_after_the_stop_that_took_it_is_cancelled_with_it`, `ur_21_s5_second_switch_refused_while_device_time_is_before_the_boundary`, `ur_23_stop_before_the_boundary_cancels_the_pending_switch`, `ur_23_stop_after_handover_before_the_boundary_takes_effect`, `ur_23_provider_stop_and_loss_cancel_the_pending_switch`, `s15_parity_provider_stop_cancels_a_pending_switch` (both Providers), `ur_22_f8_recovery_that_ends_the_repeat_cancels_the_pending_switch`, `ur_21_metadata_is_empty_or_the_mark_only`, `s15_parity_switch_mark_under_another_late_policy_is_refused_at_receipt` (the mark under another late policy is refused at receipt, both Providers), `ur_23_switch_cut_reuses_held_cut_path` and `ur_21_s9_switch_different_length_sets_the_new_period`;
  the outcome by the boundary send by `o1_uhd_t7c_a_failed_send_that_carries_the_boundary_leaves_the_switch_uncertain`, `c2_s3_a_stop_after_the_boundary_send_leaves_it_applied`, `c2_s4_a_stop_before_the_boundary_send_cancels_the_switch`, `c2_s5_a_stop_in_flight_is_decided_by_the_result`, `c2_s8_a_short_boundary_send_is_applied_when_it_took_a_sample_and_cancelled_when_it_took_none`, `c2_s10_an_underflow_after_the_boundary_send_does_not_unsend_it`, `c2_s11_a_host_late_switch_is_pending_until_the_turn_then_cancelled`, `c2_s13_a_loss_before_the_boundary_is_decided_by_device_time`, `c2_s14_provider_stop_is_decided_like_a_stop`, `c2_s15_a_failed_reopen_cancels_even_a_sent_switch`, `c2_s17b_two_accepted_switches_settle_each_on_its_own_boundary_send`, `c2_s19_a_loss_while_the_boundary_send_is_in_flight_is_decided_by_the_result_and_the_loss_sample`, `c2_s20_a_loss_after_the_boundary_send_before_device_time_reaches_b`, `c2_s21_a_stop_and_a_loss_in_flight_either_order`, `c2_s22_a_loss_after_provider_stop_in_flight_keeps_the_loss_sample`, `c2_s24_a_loss_after_a_stop_below_b_overturns_a_whole_boundary_send_s_applied`, `c2_s25_a_loss_after_the_result_of_a_send_in_flight_at_a_stop_overturns_applied`, `c2_s26_an_abandon_after_a_whole_boundary_send_halts_at_device_time`, `c2_s23_a_worker_that_panics_in_the_boundary_send_leaves_the_row_uncertain_at_the_close`, `c2_a_boundary_send_whose_worker_panicked_is_uncertain_in_the_manifest`, and `c2_no_row_is_left_pending_when_the_run_ends`.

- **UR-26** An `UpdateParameter` of a channel's frequency, gain or antenna, in either direction, is staged by `ezsdr_radio::staging::Staging::set` or refused as `COMMAND_REJECTED` ("UR-26: RM-21: <cause>", with the Action's id), by RM-21: a receive value by the stream's `Line::streaming`, a transmit value by the stream's busy end; no device call is made at its booking. A transmit burst takes the generation and the values `Staging::tx_snapshot` returns at its booking. Its owner makes a configure turn of its own before the burst and configures the device with those values (UR-12) when all of these hold: the device's generation mark is another generation, so an equal value of a new generation configures again; no device burst is open; the device time is at or after the end of the owner's last closed device burst, padding and held tail included; and no recovery is pending. The burst's late policy decides after that turn.
  The mark is the generation the device was last configured with: `arm` publishes generation 0, a configure that succeeds publishes its generation unless the device was lost during it, a configure that fails publishes none, and a loss clears it. A configure the device refuses (not a loss) refuses its burst with the burst's Action id (`COMMAND_REJECTED` of kind `tx_burst`); nothing is sent for the burst, the stream goes on, and a later burst of that generation configures again. A configure that returns `lost: true` refuses nothing: its burst stays held with its footprint (RM-15), the loss is `DEVICE_LOST`'s alone, and `Stop` or `Provider::stop` cancels the burst as any held burst. A `start_rx` takes the snapshot `Staging::start` returns at its booking, and the owner configures its segment from it (UR-12); on a stream that is streaming `Staging::start` refuses it as `COMMAND_REJECTED` of kind `command` ("UR-26: RM-21: <cause>", with the Action's id), the line unchanged and no snapshot kept.
  Any other key and a target that is neither the instance nor one of its streams are refused the same way, as is a `Stop` of another target and an Action other than `TxBurst`, `Stop` and `start_rx` on the instance's targets. A device refusal of a start's configuration is `COMMAND_REJECTED` of kind `command` with the `start_rx` Action's id; a failed `rx_stop` and a clock that cannot end are kind `stop`; a booking the timeline cannot represent carries the kind of the Action being booked.
  The instance has no `<id>/rx` or `<id>/tx` target (D14); a burst at a channel-less `<id>/tx/s<k>` and a `start_rx` at a channel-less `<id>/rx/s<k>` are as MockRadio has them (RM-29).
  A `Stop` and `Provider::stop` are RM-16, booked as UR-25 says: `Provider::stop` books the end at its instant first; a `Stop` of a receive stream node cuts that stream alone; of a transmit one, it ends the burst and keeps the clock, with its held bursts recorded only as RM-16 says.
  A `Stop` of a transmit stream whose bound does not fit its clock is refused at booking as RM-16 says, with `UR-26: the Stop's bound overflows the transmit clock` and the Action's id: no Stop is booked on the line and none reaches uhd-tx. `Provider::stop` saturates the bound.
  `Provider::stop` also sends the transmit side's end of burst, allows 100 ms **of the device host clock** (UR-2, UR-33) for its last `BURST_ACK` — a window a loss, found by any thread, ends at once (UR-29, UR-33) —, joins its threads — uhd-control, then `uhd-tx-s<k>`, then `uhd-rx-s<k>`, in stream order (design 33 D15) —, and records the stop instant in `timing`; a second `stop` does nothing. `cleanup()` stops what still runs, calls `close_streams` (every streamer) only when no thread was left detached (UR-16), writes UR-30's sections and drops what `prepare` kept (MA-7).
  *Test:* `ur_26_stop_actions`, `ur_21_a_refused_configure_refuses_its_burst_through_the_kernel`, `ur_21_a_refused_configure_refuses_the_burst_and_the_fixed_value_plays_on_the_same_clock`, `ur_25_an_equal_value_after_a_new_one_configures_once`, `ur_25_a_slow_configure_makes_a_burst_late_by_its_policy`, `ur_26_an_abort_publishes_nothing_after_the_stop_instant`, `ur_26_an_unnumbered_key_is_refused_at_runtime` (the refusal makes no device apply), `ur_26_a_device_stop_stops_every_layout_stream`, `ur_26_a_stopped_stream_keeps_its_end`, `ur_26_a_finished_stream_clears_only_its_own_floor`, `ur_26_cleanup_is_idempotent`, `ur_26_an_abort_stops_a_queued_start_when_uhd_rx_reads_the_plan`, `ur_26_uhd_rx_aborts_when_its_provider_is_gone`, `ur_26_an_orderly_stop_cuts_at_its_instant`, `ur_26_uhd_rx_follows_the_stop_it_reads`, `ur_26_the_shutdown_s_last_burst_ack_waits_on_the_device_s_clock`, `ur_26_a_second_stop_does_nothing`, `ur_26_the_sending_threads_join_before_the_receiving_ones`, `ur_26_a_receive_start_queued_by_the_stop_ends_its_owner_without_detaching`, `ur_26_shutdown_after_loss_cancels_held_bursts_record_only`, `rm_26_the_timeline_against_uhd_rx` and `ur_29_a_loss_found_by_the_shutdown_s_report_read_ends_its_ack_window`;
  a configure that returns `lost: true` by `e2b_c4_a_loss_around_the_configure`, `n98_a_lost_configure_keeps_its_booking_as_a_held_one_is_kept`, `n98_a_loss_in_the_first_bookings_configure_gives_both_held_bookings_the_note`, `n98_a_loss_landing_during_a_refusal_leaves_the_refusal`, `n98_a_provider_stop_before_a_lost_configure_completes_cancels_the_booking_once`;
  a Stop whose bound does not fit the clock by `ur_26_a_stop_whose_bound_does_not_fit_the_clock_is_refused_with_its_id_and_changes_nothing`; a switch whose boundary does not fit a clock of one tick a sample by `ur_38_a_switch_whose_boundary_does_not_fit_a_clock_of_one_tick_a_sample_is_refused_by_the_book`;
  none named for the 100 ms length of the shutdown's `BURST_ACK` window.


### Device reports

- **UR-27** When `clock_source` is not `internal`, uhd-control reads `ref_locked` every 500 ms; the first read that returns `Some(false)` emits `radio.CLOCK_LOST { reference: frequency }` (fatal, default `abort`) once, from `<id>`. A read error is recorded and does not emit. The time reference (PPS) is not monitored. The 500 ms is the device's host clock (UR-2, UR-33).
  *Test:* `ur_27_a_lost_reference_is_clock_lost`; none named for the 500 ms cadence on the host clock, the single `CLOCK_LOST`, a `ref_locked` read error recorded with no `CLOCK_LOST`, and the reference not being read when `clock_source` is `internal`.
- **UR-28** uhd-tx records every asynchronous report in the `async` section with its raw code, stream, channel and tick. A `BurstAck` marked `tx_flush: true` is recorded but does not retire a device-burst correlation. A non-ACK report of an unacknowledged flush channel, code `Underflow` or `TimeError`, read before the flush's end (every channel acknowledged, or its deadline), is recorded with `tx_flush: true` and emits no `radio.TX_UNDERFLOW`; every other report keeps its handling. A recovery attempt's `BurstAck` confirms the attempt only under UR-22's rule (the send whole, its tick at or after the attempt's floor, every channel position): an older acknowledgement is recorded and counts for nothing, and a report an attempt stashed is kept, in order, for the owner, which reads it after the recovery like any other report: it is recorded in `async` and makes its event (UR-28's rule below), and is never matched to a burst of the replacement streamer. An experiment `BurstAck(channel, t)` retires, oldest first, every pending burst on that channel whose first instant is before `t`; it touches no later target, and an ack matching none remains raw only. Without a tick, a `BurstAck` uses the first-pending rule. `Underflow` and `UnderflowInPacket` emit `radio.TX_UNDERFLOW { cause: starved }`; `SeqError` and `SeqErrorInBurst` emit `radio.TX_UNDERFLOW { cause: lost }`; both from `<id>/tx/s<k>` (RM-29) with time the report's tick in the transmit clock (floored), or `now` when it has none.
  An `Underflow` or `UnderflowInPacket` with tick `t` retires, silently, every pending burst on that channel whose first instant is at or before `t`, so a burst that underflowed is never named late; a `SeqError`, a `SeqErrorInBurst`, an abandon and an underflow report without a tick retire nothing.
  A `TimeError` report tick is radio-current-time, not the rejected packet's requested time. With tick `t`, it retires, oldest first, every pending device burst on that channel whose first instant is at or before `t`. A device burst is named by the first `TimeError` that retires any of its channels (an `Underflow` that retired a channel first marks it told, so it is never named): one `TIME_ERROR { late_at_device }` for each Kernel burst it carries (the burst that started it and each burst that joined it, RM-15), once per device burst whatever the number of channels, each with `late_by_ns` the report's root tick less the device burst's first instant, converted through its first burst's original ClockDomain, including after a cold switch or disable, and with `resumes_at` where UR-22's resume planned one for that burst (absent when the repeat ended and for a burst that is not a repeat's); a `TX_UNDERFLOW` carries `resumes_at` likewise, once, from the report that made the resume or that first reaches a burst whose resume the expired check made.
  A report that retires several device bursts at once (the dropped burst and the part that resumed it) tells each, and the newest decides the repeat by UR-22's stutter rule; the `TX_UNDERFLOW` it makes carries the newest burst's `resumes_at`. A device burst's first instant is the instant of its own start-of-burst send's first sample, which for a part that resumed a repeat is the part's `R`. A report without a tick is recorded and does nothing else.
  Each sent device burst retains one pending correlation per position in the streamer — the number `TxReport::channel` carries, which is a position in the streamer and not the device channel the stream runs (a stream opened for `{1}` reports channel 0). Reopening a streamer discards the pending correlations of its replaced report queue, and so does any other thread's open of that stream's streamer, which the record's open generation tells it (UR-36).
  `Other` is recorded only. A `tx_async` call that fails is never taken for the absence of a report (UR-3): a `lost` error is UR-29's loss, and any other ends that read, is counted in `stats.tx_errors`, and the first one's text is recorded in `rejected`.
  *Test:* `ur_22_a_flush_underflows_are_recorded_as_the_flushs`, `ur_22_t3_flush_attribution_is_per_channel_and_code_bounded`, `ur_28_an_ack_at_a_bursts_end_plus_one_tick_keeps_the_burst_at_the_next_sample`, `ur_28_underflows_are_tx_underflow`, `ur_28_the_device_reports_a_late_burst`, `ur_28_late_burst_error_and_later_ack_use_report_ticks`, `ur_28_a_time_error_names_each_burst_it_reaches_and_retires_its_own_channel`, `ur_28_a_tickless_time_error_names_and_retires_nothing`, `ur_28_a_time_error_after_an_abandoned_burst_names_the_burst_behind_its_drain`,
  `mt3_a_time_error_retires_every_pending_burst_it_reaches`, `mt3_an_underflowed_closed_burst_is_never_told_late`, `mt3_a_two_channel_burst_gets_one_event_and_one_channel_alone_retires_it`, `mt3_a_played_burst_whose_ack_was_lost_is_named_late_by_a_later_time_error`, `mt3_a_sequence_error_retires_nothing`, `mt3_a_late_device_burst_names_every_kernel_burst_joined_into_it`, `mt3_the_fake_drops_a_late_joined_device_burst_whole_and_both_bursts_are_named`, `mt3_a_joined_burst_whose_first_send_failed_is_still_named`, `mt3d_a_switch_that_joins_a_resumed_part_is_named_with_it`, `ur_28_a_finite_burst_the_device_receives_late_is_dropped_whole`, `ur_28_two_joined_bursts_a_late_report_reaches_are_both_told`, `ur_28_a_ticked_ack_does_not_retire_a_future_burst`, `ur_28_a_tickless_burst_ack_retires_only_the_first_pending_target`, `ur_28_ack_ticks_separate_a_lost_flush_ack_from_later_bursts`, `ur_28_a_failed_report_read_is_not_an_empty_one`, `ur_28_a_layout_transmit_report_names_its_stream` (the layout's source is `usrp/tx/s0`), `ur_28_a_later_stream_s_reopen_discards_only_its_correlations` (the row's `stream` is the report's) and `ur_28_a_report_is_correlated_by_its_position_in_the_streamer`,
  `ur_75_a_stale_ack_stays_for_the_report_owner_across_the_loop`, `ur_75_reports_stashed_in_an_attempt_survive_the_next_open_for_the_report_owner`, `ur_75_a_report_read_after_attempt_1_s_deadline_stays_for_the_report_owner`, `ur_75_after_three_failed_attempts_every_stash_stays_the_runs` and `ur_75_a_report_an_unconfirmed_attempt_stashed_makes_its_event_after_the_recovery` (UR-22's attempts; the acknowledgement's floor by `ur_75_an_ack_older_than_the_floor_is_not_this_attempts`); none named for the `cause` (`starved` / `lost`) of `UnderflowInPacket` and `SeqErrorInBurst`.
- **UR-29** A `DeviceError` with `lost: true` from any streaming call (`rx_recv`, `tx_send`, `tx_async`, `rx_start`, `rx_stop`, the stop on open included), or from an open's `rates` read, a `ref_locked` read at `arm`, the receive or transmit open at `arm`, a transmit owner's reopen even with a `Provider::stop` booked, or `prepare`'s configure: each loss is published before `arm` or `prepare` refuses, so `cleanup` makes no device call, and a refusal that is not a loss publishes nothing) or from uhd-control's device time read every 500 ms (which finds a lost device in a transmit-only or idle Run too), or uhd-control's device time reads failing with any error for 1 s (the device's host clock: UR-2, UR-33) from the start of the first that failed, a read that succeeds starting the count again (a failing `ref_locked` read is recorded as `timing { ref_locked_failed }` only: a device whose time it reads is not gone) (a dead link's control requests time out in UHD as `op_timeout`, which the C API returns as `UHD_ERROR_EXCEPT`, not counted lost, except at the two stream opens: `exception.hpp:229–250`, `error_c.cpp:18–35`), or a receive stream that yields only `Timeout` for 1 s after T0 + 1 s while it should be streaming (also the device's host clock: UR-17's silence monitor), makes the Provider emit `DEVICE_LOST` itself (MA-9a) once, with the error's message, and stop using the device; `stop` and `cleanup` then succeed without device calls that could block.
  **A lost device is never freed:** before the event the Provider calls `Device::mark_lost`, after which `UhdDevice` frees neither its streamers nor its `uhd_usrp` handle, leaking them — because `uhd_usrp_free` tears the RFNoC graph down, the X300 radio's `deinit()` writes its registers with no `try` (`x300_radio_control.cpp:1890–1911`), and over a dead link `poke32` throws out of `~rfnoc_graph_impl`, which ends the process before `finish` returns the Manifest (VERIFIED on the bench). `UhdDevice::drop` also keeps a device it finds not answering a time read though not marked (a dead link not yet found). A kept device goes, with its streamers, into a process-wide list; a later `UhdDevice::open` of the same `args` first reads each kept one's time — outside the list's lock, as on a dead link a read waits out UHD's timeouts — and frees those that answer, before it opens, on a live link where the teardown's writes succeed; only when no other `UhdDevice` of those `args` lives. `ezsdr_radio_uhd::uhd_kept_count()` says how many it keeps.
  What remains is UHD's: its C API keeps open devices in a function-local static map (`usrp_c.cpp:80–84`, `static.hpp:18–23`), destroyed at process exit, so a process that exits while a kept device is unreachable aborts then, after its Manifests are written. While uhd-control waits inside a UHD call on a dead link, a Session's `submit` (KC-21a) waits with it until `DEVICE_LOST`. `UhdDevice` sets `lost` for `UHD_ERROR_IO`, `UHD_ERROR_USB` and `UHD_ERROR_OS`, for `UHD_ERROR_RUNTIME` on a streaming call, and for `UHD_ERROR_EXCEPT` at `uhd_usrp_get_rx_stream` and `uhd_usrp_get_tx_stream` only; a receive metadata error other than UR-18…UR-19's (`BROKEN_CHAIN` among them) is an error, not a lost device (INFERRED: `RUNTIME` is generic).
  *Test:* `ur_29_a_lost_device_aborts_the_run`, `ur_29_a_silent_stream_is_a_lost_device` (`DEVICE_LOST` 1 to 2 s after the silence begins), `ur_29_a_lost_device_is_found_while_idle` and `ur_16_a_panicking_thread_is_a_lost_device` (each also asserting that the device is marked lost once and before any `close_streams`), `ur_29_a_lost_later_start_is_published_before_the_owners_are_stopped` (a lost `rx_start` in `start` is published before the owners it began are stopped, and no device call follows it), `ur_29_a_loss_between_two_channels_of_a_configure_applies_no_more` (a loss published after one channel's apply stops the next channel's), `ur_29_a_dead_link_that_fails_without_lost_is_a_lost_device` (a transmit-only Session on `FakeFault::Unreachable`: `DEVICE_LOST` 1–2 s after the link dies), `ur_29_time_reads_that_fail_apart_are_not_a_lost_device` (`FakeFault::TimeReadFailsFor` twice, 1.5 s apart), `ur_29_a_failing_reference_sensor_alone_is_not_a_lost_device` (`FakeFault::RefLockedFails`) and `ur_29_a_loss_while_the_start_is_queued_makes_stop_stop_nothing_more` (a loss while a start is queued 2.1 s ahead, then `Provider::stop`: one `DEVICE_LOST` and no `rx_stop` after the loss);
  `ur_29_a_lost_transmit_open_at_arm_is_published_and_cleanup_closes_nothing`, `ur_29_a_lost_transmit_open_after_the_receive_open_at_arm_is_published_once`, `ur_29_a_lost_reference_read_at_arm_is_published_before_any_open` and `ur_29_a_lost_rate_read_at_arm_is_published_and_the_streamer_is_not_closed` (the loss is published once before `arm` refuses, and after the lost call only `mark_lost` is called, `cleanup` twice included), `ur_29_a_lost_rate_read_is_the_devices_own_error_and_is_not_cached`, `ur_29_an_open_whose_rate_read_is_lost_closes_no_streamer`, `ur_29_a_reopen_whose_rate_read_is_lost_closes_no_streamer` and `ur_29_a_receive_owner_open_whose_rate_read_is_lost_publishes_it_and_closes_no_streamer` (a lost rate read is the device's error, holds no outcome and no close follows it), `ur_29_a_lost_apply_in_prepare_is_published_and_refused_with_the_devices_text`, `ur_29_a_lost_reopen_while_a_stop_is_booked_is_published` and `ur_29_a_loss_at_prepare_or_arm_ends_the_run_as_a_lost_receive_open_does` (one delivered `DEVICE_LOST` in the Manifest at `prepare`, the reference read, each open and the rate read), and, with the non-lost twins `ur_13_a_refusal_at_arm_that_is_not_a_loss_is_unchanged`, `ur_12_a_refused_apply_in_prepare_that_is_not_a_loss_publishes_nothing` and `ur_22_a_refused_reopen_while_a_stop_is_booked_reports_nothing`; for the classification `uhd_47_is_lost_only_at_the_two_stream_opens` and `uhd_47_at_a_stream_open_is_a_lost_device` (feature `uhd`);
  `ur_34_a_device_loss_waits_for_an_admitted_streamer_open_and_close` (a streamer open or close in progress completes before loss publication, and neither is called after `mark_lost`), `ur_34_a_rate_read_waiting_for_device_gate_does_not_call_after_loss` (a rate read waiting for the lifecycle gate makes no call after loss publication), `ur_29_a_lost_receive_owner_configure_releases_stream_lock_before_reporting`; the server's `ea_07_a_lost_device_ends_the_session_with_its_manifest`; none named for the kept-device list and for a receive metadata error other than UR-18…UR-19's (`BROKEN_CHAIN`) being an error and not a lost device; the `UHD_ERROR_IO` case by `uhd_tx_async_reports_a_failed_call_as_an_error` (feature `uhd`).

### Records

- **UR-30** The Provider's Manifest sections (MA-10) are, under `ezsdr.radio.uhd.`, each filed by the Kernel under the instance's source root (RS-39), so no id is in a name: `device` (`describe()`: `args`, master clock rate, channel counts, `pp_string`, UHD's version, and `fake: true` for `FakeDevice`; and, from the opening checks, `serial`, `subdev_spec` — each direction's markup, `null` for one with no element — and `rates_checked` — `rx` and `tx`, both fields, true for a direction whose rates were compared, at its first open (UR-13), false for one whose rates read was lost); `envelope` (the profile's `RadioEnvelope`, RM-20); `applied` (every applied setting: key, claimed value, read-back, instant; each row's key is the concrete channel key — `radio.<d>.c<n>.frequency_hz` — and a configure's row names it in `channel` too, a rate's is its stream's key — `radio.<d>.s<k>.sample_rate_hz` —, and an antenna's row reads the antenna back).
  `bursts` (SC-28's records, in order, each the host's view of what it handed over: a burst the device then dropped late is in `async` and in a `TIME_ERROR { late_at_device }`, not marked in the record, whose type is the Kernel's); `async` (UR-28); `rejected` (every `COMMAND_REJECTED` reason, thread failure, bounded-join timeout, leaked streamer and the first failed `tx_async` read (UR-28)); `switches` (one row per accepted repeat switch, RM-37: its action, predecessor, receipt instant, both periods, `boundary` (the one it settled at, UR-38) and `outcome`);
  `timing` (arm, T0 and its lead, the first receive block's index and its host delay after `start` (real time), restarts with their instants, failed device time reads, a repeat's resumes (`tx_resume`: its stream, the cause, the handed cursor, the resume point and the owner's instant, UR-22; the `bursts` section then holds one record per part), stop instants (a transmit `Stop` as it was applied, `tx_stop_applied`: its stream, its root instant and the root tick its bookings end at), and, written once by `Provider::stop` after it has joined uhd-control and not at all when `start` was refused before uhd-control was started, the plans the streams were carried out by, the one each stream's threads share (UR-25): `plan`, each segment's stream, the arrival order of the command that began it (`null` for a segment no command began), its origin, end, logical channels in block order and root ticks per sample); `stats` (`rx_blocks`, `rx_samples`, `rx_overflows`, `rx_errors`, `rx_off_lattice`, `rx_overlapping`, `link_drops_seen`, `tx_bursts`, `tx_samples`, `tx_errors`; and once uhd-tx has sent, `tx_lock_wait_max_ns`, the longest host-clock wait for the streams lock in a send's plan or result).
  `device` and `envelope` are set at construction; the others at `cleanup`.
  *Test:* `ur_30_the_sections_are_written`, `e2a_a_send_records_the_longest_lock_wait` and `core_stat_max_keeps_the_largest` (`tx_lock_wait_max_ns`, unit), `ur_30_the_device_section_names_the_subdev_spec`, `ur_30_the_device_section_records_the_serial`, `ur_30_the_device_section_records_the_rate_check`, `ur_30_every_applied_key_is_declared_by_the_instance` (the opening checks' records, sealed in a Run's Manifest; and every `applied` row's key one the instance declares), `ur_30_the_plan_names_every_stream_at_the_stop` (exactly one `plan` row, its stream, command and logical channels, and the stop's end per stream), `ur_16_a_uhd_control_panic_after_it_returns_does_not_write_a_second_plan_row` (one row when uhd-control panics after its loop returned), `ur_15_a_failed_rx_owner_spawn_abandons_its_started_chain_before_cleanup` (no row for a refused start), `ur_30_a_transmit_stop_records_the_bound_its_bookings_end_at` (the `tx_stop_applied` row's stream, instant and bound), `s15_parity_untimed_booking_after_stop` (the UHD region is 10 ms past the Stop, and no repeat starts before its region's end); `ur_05_the_layout_builds_the_subdev_spec` reads the device section's `rx_channels`; none named for the section's `args`, master clock rate, transmit channel count, `pp_string` and `uhd_version`.
- **UR-31** `UhdDevice::fidelity()` is `real` on every aspect (MA-42); `FakeDevice::fidelity()` is `Fidelity::NONE`, so a Run on the fake device records `none` and never claims `real`.
  *Test:* `ur_31_fidelity_follows_the_device` (the fake records `none`) and `hw_b3_receive_at_t0` (the `UhdDevice` is `real` on every aspect; run on a USRP).
- **UR-32** The receive path allocates no buffer per block once warm: `HostPool` slots are reused when no block references them (HD-2), and `rx_recv`'s per-channel vectors are reused by `UhdDevice`. The receive path does not meet this today: `UhdDevice::rx_recv` allocates its vectors per call (§10).
  *Producer obligation.*

### The test double and the bench

- **UR-33** `FakeDevice` is a double of a USRP, compiled always and constructed only by Rust code (`FakeDevice::new(FakeConfig)`); `open` never returns one. It is faithful to the device in the ways the tests of §6 depend on, and each way is named here so that a test cannot pass on a looser fake: **time** — it counts ticks of a configurable master clock (default 200 MHz) from its host clock `HostClock`, the real one by default or the manual one `FakeConfig::clock` selects (UR-2, UR-33), running `FakeConfig::drift_ppm` (default 0) fast or slow against the host; it counts from that clock's reading at its **own creation**, so a device built later has not already spent time and its fault deadlines count from its creation; `set_time_zero` resets the count (at the next whole second of its clock when `at_next_pps`). **Transmit** — a closed undersized first packet (`sob` and `eob`, 1–3 samples per channel, but not one whose one-sample EOB lost its acknowledgement,
  which keeps its stale-header rule) is timed: a TimeError per channel, the device burst dropped through its EOB, no BurstAck; untimed: one or more Underflows, then the BurstAck (counts not pinned). An open first packet (`sob` without `eob`) of 1–29 samples per channel at interpolation 80 or more arms its device burst: the first later packet at a later device tick (a packet at the arming tick clears it), or a read that finds the burst dry, gives one Underflow per channel at the burst's cursor; the first later packet gets a TimeError per channel, with no BurstAck; each later timed packet (one that carries its own time spec) is refused with a TimeError, an untimed one once, and the packets after it through the EOB are dropped with no report.
  A BurstAck's tick is the burst's exclusive end plus one master tick. A timed `sob` less than `g` samples after the end of a closed device burst of at most 32 samples, or less than three after a longer one (burst length counted as in the next paragraph), is refused while the radio is idle, as the idle rule below (`g` is `max(3, ceil(15 µs × the stream's rate))`, written again in the fake): a TimeError per channel, the device burst dropped through its EOB, no BurstAck.
  The short-burst guard counts every sample the host sent in that device burst's packets, not only its last packet's, refused or dropped packets included (the DUC consumes them before the radio applies packet policy).
  That is the transmitted count unless a fault dropped packets; then it can be larger and select the three-sample gap (INFERRED: no faulted burst was measured). A short burst abandoned while open, and an acknowledged untimed four-zero flush (UR-22), are guarded from their exclusive ends, the front `g` samples past them. A `sob` refused by the short-burst guard refuses its device burst: each later timed packet is refused with a `TimeError`, the first later untimed one too, and the rest are dropped through the EOB with no report (INFERRED: the hardware's behaviour for that refusal is not measured). Only an accepted burst's dropped close arms the guard, so a refused `sob`'s EOB arms none and the guard of the closed burst before it still holds. The guard models UR-22's front, not the device: the sweep of 2026-11-10 needed at most about 10 µs after a closed short head at interpolation 60 to 400 and one sample at 4 to 50, and two or three samples after any close at 100 and 200 MS/s (VERIFIED at the rates measured; INFERRED elsewhere), and the fake applies `g` at every interpolation and three samples after every longer close, so it is stricter than the device and never looser at the rates the Module allows, 1 MS/s and up (at 0.4 MS/s and below the bench saw closed bursts of at most four samples underflow themselves, and at 0.25 MS/s and below a two-zero flush was not acknowledged; the guard models neither).
  The Module never books inside the guard, since its own front is the guard's end. The run of a long burst after a short head (`hw_n47s5_long_b_after_a_short_head_gap_below_and_at_the_front`) is the bench's check at each rate. No flush fault is modelled.
  **channels** — a front end table per direction: each slot's board (`FakeConfig::front_ends`, slot A first, default `["UBX", "UBX"]`, at most two) contributes UHD's front ends — a Basic/LF board's receive side `0` and `1`, its transmit side `0`, every other board `0` each way (`db_basic_and_lf.hpp`'s `rx_frontends`/`tx_frontends`) — whose `front_end` is UHD's name of the entry: the board's name followed by ` RX` or ` TX`, and `BasicRX (0)` for a Basic/LF board (`db_basic_and_lf.cpp:101-111`). The direction's channels are its current subdev spec, the table at construction and `set_subdev_spec`'s afterwards (UHD's markup, `"A:0 B:0"`); a settings change to a front end that stays keeps its values, a new one starts at its defaults (INFERRED). `set_subdev_spec` refuses a pair no table entry matches, in UHD's words (`LookupError: Could not find radio on mboard 0 that matches subdev A:1`), a markup of three or more `:`-parts (`ValueError: invalid subdev-spec markup string: …`, `subdev_spec.cpp:58-59`), with UHD's tokenization, tokens split on ASCII spaces alone (`subdev_spec.cpp:19-20`), and a repeated pair and an empty spec (stricter than UHD, INFERRED; `multi_usrp_rfnoc.cpp:2796`). The tests' one-CBX fake is `["CBX-120", "Unknown (0xffff) - 0"]`, as a real X310 with slot B empty.
  **streamers** — one per direction and stream number may be open, counted from 0, each with its own channel list (`rx_open(stream, channels)`), start, stop and packets and its own asynchronous report queue, as UHD's are (`uhd_tx_streamer_recv_async_msg` is a streamer call); the graph refuses a second streamer on a channel one already holds (`Attempting to reconnect output port!` for a receive channel and `… input port!` for a transmit one, `graph.cpp:179-197`, the same channel twice in one list included), while a stream's own reopen replaces its predecessor and a refused reopen leaves the stream with none, as `UhdDevice`'s does (UR-25);
  An open of a front end another stream's streamer holds is refused and makes the fake's drop fail the test unless the test takes it (`take_abort`): UHD 4.10 refuses it too, but cuts the holder's edge and then aborts the process. `rx_close(stream)` and `tx_close(stream)` remove that stream's streamer reference, no-op when it is absent, and FakeDevice records the close; `close_streams` frees every streamer of both directions; a call naming a stream no streamer is open for is answered as `UhdDevice` answers it, but two of them wait on the host clock first (with no fault bouncing the call earlier): `rx_packet_samples` is 0 and `rx_start`, `rx_stop` and `tx_send` fail at once, while `rx_recv` fails after at most 10 ms of its host clock and `tx_async` finds no report after its timeout, where `UhdDevice` answers every one at once (UR-3); and `tx_send` refuses a send whose buffers are not its streamer's channels, or any shorter than the first, as `UhdDevice` refuses them before its C call (UR-3). An empty channel list is channel `0`, as UHD reads it (`sanitize_stream_args`): the streamer opened with none is channel 0's — one receive vector, one transmit buffer —, and the open holds channel 0 like any other.
  Setting a direction's subdev spec disconnects that direction's open streamers, as UHD's rebuild of its channel map does (`_set_subdev_spec`'s "Disconnect and clear existing chains"), and its channels count as unconnected again. `rates` reports the profile's rate grid as UHD's `(start, stop, step)` ranges — `(r, r, 0.0)` for each of the 639 rates — except that a `rates_before_open` fake reports `[(1.0, 1.0, 0.0)]` for a channel no streamer has opened since its direction's spec was last set (INFERRED); `serial` is `FakeConfig::serial`.
  **configuration** — `apply` clips a rate to `mcr / N` for the decimation `N` nearest the requested `mcr / rate` (UHD's `coerce_decim`, ties to the smaller `N`; design 33 D5), a gain to 0.5 dB steps and applies a frequency exactly, taking `FakeConfig::apply_delay` (default 0) of its host clock's time (a clock wait, not a wall one); its `Applied.antenna` is the antenna the channel then holds when the change named one, and `None` otherwise (design 33 D16).
  **DSP** — each front end has a DDC (receive) or DUC (transmit) chain whose frequency is logged by tick: an untimed frequency write is the value at once, and its log entry is at the tick it was made; a sample's value is the chain's at its own tick, so a later write does not change what was produced; `read_back` reports the value so far. A chain is the front end's and outlives a streamer (INFERRED).
  A timed TX burst advances the DUC sample-count timeline from its first timed header, and every timed continuation keeps `has_time`; an untimed continuation clears it. The last timed header survives streamer close/reopen; an untimed SOB/EOB flush clears that header; a one-sample SOB/EOB loses its ack and leaves the next timed header ignored until reset. `freq_at` observes the chain.
   **transmit** — a transmit queue in order: samples are accepted at most 50 ms ahead of its clock (blocking otherwise). The DUC consumes each packet first: while `has_time`, it advances the burst timestamp by the sample count; the DUC burst ends at its EOB word even if the downstream radio later drops that packet. When the DUC starts a timed burst, its first header supplies the packet timestamp; thereafter packet header times — including an active timed start-of-burst — do not restart it.
   While the radio is idle, every packet's DUC-generated time is checked, not only a start-of-burst: a time in the past or at or before the end of samples already queued is reported `TimeError` and that packet is dropped (at the end: the X300's behaviour), reporting a tick at or after the late time, and a future packet starts playback with or without start-of-burst (except a packet of a device burst whose head was dropped late, below). A timed start-of-burst met while the radio is active is not time-checked and does not reset the DUC timeline. An open burst that runs out of samples before its end-of-burst is reported `Underflow` and the radio becomes idle, while its DUC burst timeline persists until its EOB word. A start-of-burst without a time spec inside a burst is recorded as a restart (the CORDIC reset UHD performs, RM-13's evidence), and an accepted `BurstAck` is reported at its burst's exclusive end plus one master tick (UR-33); a one-sample SOB/EOB loses its acknowledgement and the next timed start is ignored, while an untimed two-zero SOB/EOB is acknowledged.
   A device burst whose head the idle radio drops late is dropped whole: its later packets are refused whatever their own times, as a refused `sob`'s are above (a timed one with a TimeError per channel, the first later untimed one too, the rest silent through the EOB), and no BurstAck follows (VERIFIED in the FPGA source and on an X310 at 1 MS/s with one 20 000-sample send, 2026-11-13; INFERRED for chunked sends, other rates and one channel late alone).
   The DUC consumes the dropped packets one after another at one sample per `ratio` ticks, `ratio` being the master ticks of a sample of the stream's rate. A dropped packet is read at the latest of the device's time, the radio's cursor, the tick the drain's samples have reached and its own time, and its TimeErrors carry that tick, which can be ahead of the fake's clock. An idle timed packet, with or without a `sob`, before the drain's limit is late too, is dropped through its EOB and lengthens the drain; the limit is the tick the drain's samples reach, plus `pad` samples, plus `g`, with `pad = ceil(S / 100) + 2 × P + K × (g + ceil(10 µs × rate))`, `S` the samples, `P` the sends and `K` the device bursts (closed by their EOB) the drain has taken. A drain begins at a late drop at or after the previous limit, survives the reopen of its stream (the FPGA keeps draining) and ends at `set_time_zero`.
   The pad and `g` are the fake's own margin, and the fake is stricter than the X310 by construction: a closely spaced train of bursts behind a late one stays dropped while its drain lasts. Each term is INFERRED until bench cells C3 and C4 measure it (the 1 % share of the samples, two clocks a send, `g` plus 10 µs a burst), the host-to-radio delay is not modelled, and no test pins a device value.
   A short head that keeps the DUC on short output packets is declared, not modelled: the 2 per send covers it only as far as INFERRED.
   A guard refusal, an undersized packet and an armed burst's rest do not drain: the fake is looser than the X310 there (INFERRED), and no Module shape reaches them (an open first send is at least 32 samples, a short close is padded to 4 and the gap kept).
   Not modelled: flow control of dropped packets, the device's 32-entry error queue and its report spacing; an untimed packet inside a drain plays at once (looser than the X310), and the first untimed continuation after a late head is refused where the X310 plays it late (stricter; bench 2026-11-13, H1's second cell).
  **receive** — for each receive sample instant it returns the sample transmitted on transmit channel `c` at that instant (delayed by `FakeConfig::loopback_delay_samples`, default 0) **only when that receive channel's and that transmit channel's frequencies are equal and the transmit channel's rate matches**, and otherwise a ramp `(k mod 65 536) / 65 536` on the real part; it paces `rx_recv` to its clock; it reports `Overflow` when the reader falls more than 100 ms behind (then skips to the next sample instant after its clock, as a restarted stream would), `LateCommand` for a receive start in its past; it stops the stream at once on `rx_stop`, as the X3x0 does, still delivering what it produced before the stop, ahead of the next stream's samples (the bench's one or two blocks); an `rx_recv` on a stopped stream waits its whole timeout, as UHD's does; with `FakeConfig::rx_latency` and `rx_packet` (defaults 0 and a whole request) it delivers a packet that long after its last sample and waits for each packet up to the timeout, returning what it has when one does not come, the next call waiting as usual (UHD caches no timeout: `rx_streamer_impl.hpp:25–47`), its packets on the stream's own grid from its start, a sample there once the packet holding it is, as UHD's `recv` does; an empty end-of-burst it takes as one zero sample, as UHD sends it (`rx_streamer_impl.hpp`).
  **a queued start** — an `rx_stop` before the origin of a timed start still queued cancels it, so nothing of it arrives, while `rx_close`, `close_streams` and a replacing `rx_open` of a streamer that holds such a start do not: the start stays queued per device channel, and a later `rx_open` of that channel adopts it (the old start's blocks arrive on the reopened streamer at the old origin) until an `rx_stop` of that streamer, the origin's passing, `set_time_zero` or a receive `set_subdev_spec` drops it (VERIFIED on an X310, 2026-11-13, one channel at 1, 10 and 100 MS/s, the stop and the close each; INFERRED: the keying by channel whatever the stream number and channel order, a close and reopen that spans the origin or changes the rate, and a stop of the reopened streamer cancelling it).
  **transmit** — it counts a timed start-of-burst sent inside an un-ended burst (`unended_bursts`), `transmitted` returns what it transmitted at given instants, and it records `mark_lost` among its calls; its reports reach `tx_async` in order, a burst's `BurstAck` only once its end has played, as the device sends it then, so that a later burst's report waits behind it (VERIFIED: UHD 4.10's `radio_tx_core.v:371–374` acknowledges end-of-burst in `ST_TRANSMIT` as the last sample goes out, and judges a later packet's time only back in `ST_IDLE`, `:348–355`), and a read that finds none waits its timeout whether or not a streamer is open.
   A test scripts faults on it (`FakeConfig`): a stall of the reader, an overflow or a sequence error at an instant, an alignment error, an underflow report, a device loss (every later call fails with `lost: true`), or one at a named call (`LostOnCall(call, nth)`: the `nth` `apply`, `rates`, `ref_locked`, `rx_open` or `tx_open` and every call after it), suppressed TX BurstAck reports (`DropBurstAck`; `DropAckOfSend(n)` suppresses those of one send only), a `tx_send` that blocks forever only when timed (`TxBlocks`), a `tx_send` that takes only half its samples and not its end-of-burst (`ShortSend`, UHD's `send` timing out part way — half, where UHD stops at a whole packet, which no test depends on), one that does the same and then fails, a one-sample send taking nothing (`FailSend`, not known of UHD) and one that takes nothing (`StalledSend`), and a `set_sources` that fails with UHD's lock-failure text (`ReferenceDoesNotLock`).
   It also scripts a dead link whose calls fail without `lost` (`Unreachable`: control calls wait 100 ms and fail, receives time out, sends take nothing), time reads failing for a while (`TimeReadFailsFor`) and a reference sensor that fails to read (`RefLockedFails`), a scripted TX open refusal (`TxOpenFails`) or a non-profile rate set at one `rates` read (`RatesMismatch`), each named by its relevant call index, a failing time read, a slow one (5 ms, UR-7's bracket rule), an unlocked reference, and a rate it applies wrongly (at `prepare` or at a later switch), and an antenna a channel reads back as another name (`WrongAntenna`, design 33 D16's read-back check). Its serial is `FakeConfig::serial`, one per instance by default, so that two default fakes are two devices. `describe()` says `fake: true`; `FakeDevice` records every call for the tests to read.
  **The host clock (UR-2).** The real clock is real time; the manual one follows M1–M7. **M1**: a participant is a thread the uhd crate itself starts (`HostClock::spawn` registers it before it runs, a guard unregisters it when the thread ends, a panic included, and a start that fails registers nothing) or a thread that registers itself with `HostClock::drive()` (its guard unregisters it when dropped, a panic included); a thread is registered at most once on a manual clock, a second `drive()` on it is refused before anything is registered, and a real clock registers nothing. **M2**: a participant is idle only while it waits on the clock — a sleep, a clock condition wait or the clock lock — and busy otherwise, a standard mutex included. **M3**: while any participant is busy the clock stops. **M4**: a waiting participant's deadline stops it there until that participant runs; a participant woken by a notify or handed the clock lock is busy at that instant too. **M5**: while every participant is idle the clock advances no faster than real time and never takes back what it lost while busy. **M6**: a non-participant thread (the Kernel's, or a test's that does not drive the clock) waits on the same clock but stops nothing. **M7**: `sleep(d)` ends only once the clock has advanced by `d`; no notify or spurious wake ends it early.
  A shared lock a participant holds across a device call or a clock wait is the **clock lock**: a waiting participant is idle and the holder hands it over inside the clock, making the waiter busy at the hand-off. A participant that measures clock time across a loop therefore waits on the clock in every pass, or it stops the very time it measures — a device read that failed at once waits its timeout there (UR-28's failed `tx_async` read included).

    `mt3_c1_rehearsal_the_fake_plays_the_on_time_control_and_drops_the_late_chunked_burst_whole` (the Module's chunk shape late behind an on-time control, with the same cell on the bench's `hw_mt3_c1_chunked_late_burst`; its verdict by `mt3_c1_verdict_passes_with_complete_evidence`, `mt3_c1_verdict_absent_receive_is_inconclusive_not_pass`, `mt3_c1_verdict_incomplete_receive_is_inconclusive`, `mt3_c1_verdict_without_the_control_is_inconclusive`, `mt3_c1_verdict_a_receive_outcome_inside_the_range_is_inconclusive`, `mt3_c1_verdict_a_heard_interval_inside_the_late_interval_fails`, `mt3_c1_verdict_a_heard_interval_that_straddles_the_late_interval_fails`, `mt3_c1_verdict_a_burst_ack_attributed_by_tick`, `mt3_c1_verdict_a_last_chunk_handed_after_its_own_time_is_inconclusive` and `mt3_c1_verdict_without_a_time_error_is_inconclusive`; its tone detector by `mt3_c1_the_tone_detector_hears_a_tone_and_neither_noise_nor_a_ramp`),
    `ur_33_a_timed_sob_met_while_a_burst_plays_does_not_restart_it` and `ur_33_a_one_sample_eob_loses_the_next_timed_header` (the radio/DUC packet rules), the open-short and closed-undersized rules (`ur_33_an_open_first_packet_of_29_samples_faults`, `ur_33_the_open_short_rule_starts_at_interpolation_80`, `ur_33_a_short_open_send_at_10_mhz_is_clean`, `ur_33_an_open_first_send_of_30_is_clean`, `ur_33_a_short_open_send_continued_at_the_same_tick_is_clean`, `ur_33_a_refused_sob_packet_does_not_arm_the_short_rule`, `ur_33_a_new_burst_s_sob_clears_the_short_rule`, `ur_33_an_open_short_burst_reports_one_underflow_per_channel`, `ur_33_a_read_that_finds_the_short_burst_dry_is_its_one_underflow`, `ur_33_a_delayed_timed_open_burst_underflows_with_no_ack`, `ur_33_a_delayed_untimed_open_burst_underflows_with_no_ack`, `ur_33_a_timed_open_burst_with_untimed_continuations_reports_once_per_channel`, `ur_33_a_closed_four_sample_packet_is_not_dropped`), plus the D9 differential probe `ur_33_d9_raw_probes_match_the_x3xx_tx_model`.

  the queued start by `ur_33_an_untimed_stop_before_a_queued_origin_cancels_it`, `ur_33_a_close_and_reopen_does_not_cancel_a_queued_start`, `ur_33_a_stop_before_the_close_or_on_the_reopened_streamer_cancels_the_orphan`, `ur_33_a_reopen_on_other_channels_inherits_nothing`, `ur_33_an_orphan_is_adopted_per_channel_whatever_the_stream_number`, `ur_33_an_orphan_does_not_outlive_its_origin_a_time_zero_or_a_spec_change`, `ur_33_a_start_queued_behind_an_unread_tail_survives_a_close_and_reopen` and `ur_33_a_close_of_a_stream_that_has_begun_leaves_no_start_for_a_reopen`, and, with the same cells on the bench's `hw_67_stop_before_origin` (control, stop, close, close then stop), `iii_n67_cancel_probe_rehearses_on_the_fake`,
