- **UR-34** `tests/hardware.rs` (feature `uhd`; every test `#[ignore = "needs a USRP: see plan/phase7/bench.md"]`; the device from `EZSDR_UHD_ARGS`, the binding's layout the described boards its front ends name — a `cbx` element for `CBX…`, an `obx` element for `OBX…`, a `ubx` element for `UBX…`, a channel whose front end names no board the profile describes left out — and the steps' frequency that board's default) runs the bench's steps: `hw_b1_probe`, `hw_b2_authority`, `hw_b3_receive_at_t0`, `hw_b4_capture_at_a_sample_index`, `hw_b5_overflow`, `hw_b6_txrx_and_repeat`, `hw_b7_session_loopback`, `hw_b8_leads`, `hw_b9_unplug` (manual), `hw_b9_usrp2_probe`, `hw_measurements_record` and `hw_h4_load`. Steps B3, B4, B6 and B7 call the same functions as `tests/fake.rs`'s rehearsal cases, and B5 the one the unit test `rehearsal_b5_overflow` calls, with a `UhdDevice` in place of the `FakeDevice`; B3, B5, B6 and B7 print the records `bench-results.md` needs, and B4 asserts its capture index. `hw_measurements_record` runs the Provider-path timing, overflow and throughput measurements and raw coercion read-back measurements; it restores the unstreamed settings afterwards unless the raw group stopped, and writes the record before profile comparison. `hw_b1_probe` prints every channel's front end and its transmit frequency and gain as the device reports them, before any transmitting step, and checks the profile's channels and front ends as UR-5 does.
  `tests/fake.rs` runs the rehearsals of B3, B4, B6 and B7 on a one-OBX fake as well (`rehearsal_*_on_one_obx`, under an OBX layout), and B3 on a one-CBX fake at 2.45 GHz. B5 provokes a real overflow with `UhdRadio::with_rx_stall(after, duration)`, a builder only Rust code reaches (like `FakeDevice`), which makes uhd-rx-s0 stop calling `rx_recv` once for `duration`, `after` its first block — `after` and `duration` are the device's host clock (UR-2, UR-33) (the start is UR-15's two seconds before the first sample).
  For measurement only, Rust bench code may call `UhdRadio::with_measurement_timing(timing)` before assembly to replace the Description's timing with candidate values and update the capability tree, `min_command_lead` and envelope section; ordinary construction remains profile-based.
  Likewise `UhdRadio::with_measurement_performance(performance)` replaces the Description's PerformanceEnvelope and rebuilds the same surfaces, so that a measurement Run is admitted under a candidate link ceiling (RM-32); the ceiling is an admission bound, not the link's measured capacity, and ordinary construction keeps the profile's link.
  *Test:* `the_candidate_ceiling_admits_what_the_profiles_link_limit_refuses`.
  *Test:* `hw_b1_probe`, `hw_b2_authority`, `hw_b3_receive_at_t0`, `hw_b4_capture_at_a_sample_index`, `hw_b5_overflow`, `hw_b6_txrx_and_repeat`, `hw_b7_session_loopback`, `hw_b8_leads`, `hw_b9_unplug`, `hw_b9_usrp2_probe`, `hw_measurements_record`; `hw_b1_probe` checks the layout's channels and front ends against the board of the device's first receive front end as UR-5 does, any board the profile describes.
  B3, B4, B6 and B7 call the same functions as `rehearsal_b3_receive_at_t0`, `rehearsal_b4_capture_at_a_sample_index`, `rehearsal_b6_txrx_and_repeat` and `rehearsal_b7_session_loopback` in `tests/fake.rs`; B5's `rehearse_overflow` has its fake-side case in the unit test `rehearsal_b5_overflow` in `src/provider/rx.rs`, on the controlled clock; B3, B5, B6 and B7 print the records `bench-results.md` needs, and B4 asserts its capture index and prints nothing.
  Candidate timing's six fields and Run Manifest propagation by `measurement_candidate_propagates_all_timing_fields_to_the_run_manifest` and `measurement_timing_candidate_updates_every_surface_and_the_start_boundary`; the remaining readers by `measurement_candidate_admission_lead_does_not_override_the_device_lead`, `ur_17_the_missed_start_lead_is_measured_from_the_decision_s_now`, `ur_21_tx_reads_in_flight_window_from_description` and `measurement_candidate_in_flight_window_sets_the_tx_chunk_size`;
  the fake rehearsal by `measurement_fake_rehearsal_round_trips_and_only_fails_source`, and the two-provider source rejection by `fake_source_is_rejected_by_both_providers_only_for_source`; the raw hardware record entry point by `hw_measurements_record` (ignored, run with a USRP and complete measurement metadata).
  Provider measurement groups and their record derivation by `manifest_evidence_extracts_six_classes_and_rejects_boundary_acknowledgements`, `cold_capture_evidence_requires_two_contiguous_clock_segments`, `throughput_success_requires_sample_tolerance_and_no_overflow`, `overflow_evidence_requires_one_overrun_one_matching_gap_and_the_measured_rate`, `overflow_restart_after_resume_estimate_uses_gap_end_and_nulls_negative_results`, `overflow_summary_keeps_observations_and_the_key_is_not_a_record_value`, `tx_throughput_uses_the_burst_target_ack_elapsed_and_minimum_samples`, `loopback_success_separates_waveform_from_requested_sample_time`, `measured_rows_keep_the_selected_boundary_and_limiting_condition` and `provider_measurement_groups_fake_rehearsal_round_trips_without_claiming_measurements`; the hardware entry point remains `hw_measurements_record`.
  `measurement_group_merges_only_supplied_comparable_values`, `measurement_plan_from_env_uses_defaults_and_rejects_invalid_counts` and `measurement_defaults_restoration_stops_at_the_first_apply_error`.
  *Test:* `measurement_plan_from_env_uses_defaults_and_rejects_invalid_counts` (the defaults, floors and variables of the step, overflow, throughput and loopback counts).
  The coercion read-back values and the profile-grid inclusion by `coercion_readbacks_keep_only_exact_gain_rate_and_antenna_values` and `gain_grid_comparison_rejects_a_profile_point_missing_from_readback`.
  Stage 3c-2b's observation probes by `hw_h1_late_multi_packet_burst`, `hw_h2_underflow_mid_burst`, `hw_h5_held_last_sample`, `hw_56_stop_between_starts`, `hw_56_stop_lag` and `hw_ur17_receive_call_duration`; their calculations by `heard_intervals_cover_silence_midstream_and_a_truncated_tail`, `correlation_offset_finds_zero_positive_negative_and_noisy_offsets`, `correlation_offset_marks_equal_peaks_ambiguous` and `report_counts_include_all_codes_and_malformed_rows`; and forwarding-Device evidence by `successful_tx_eob_requires_an_accepted_eob`, `receive_sample_follows_a_successful_device_start` and `drain_without_reader_resumes_after_rx_panic`.
  Stage 3c-2c: the raw group (coercion, then the restore) shares one failure latch, looked at before every untimed `apply` of its sweeps and restore: after the first `apply` error, or past its wall-clock limit, it sends nothing more and restores nothing, and marks every key it had not finished `unmeasured` with the reason (a value read back before the stop stays). The group's state is read after the restore, and the record run leaks the device handle (it does not drop it) when the group, its restore or an earlier group failed, also when the record's writing unwinds, so that UHD's teardown does not touch the control port.
  `hw_measurements_record` opens, describes and identifies the device before any group, runs each raw probe and group on its own (an error or a panic leaves that group's keys `unmeasured`) and writes the record before profile comparison; a probe's arrays of more than 20 rows are summarised in the record and written whole to `<OUTPUT>.rows/<probe>.jsonl`. A Spec Run that validate or prepare refuses is an `AdmissionRejection` for its rate. The test helper retries opening the Authority only when the reference clock's PLL did not lock, and a raw probe's `set_sources` takes the same retries; when they are used up inside a record, the record's latch is set, every later probe and group is `not run` and the record is written.
  *Test:* `the_pll_retries_used_up_latch_the_record_and_nothing_more_is_opened`.
  Each channel's `frequency_hz` and `frequency_step_hz` are measured only when every request over the profile grid's ends and sampled points reads back within 0.05 Hz.
  *Test:* `raw_group_sends_nothing_after_its_first_failed_apply`, `coercion_stops_after_its_first_failed_apply`, `coercion_stopped_midway_keeps_what_it_measured_and_marks_the_rest_unmeasured`, `coercion_opens_no_streamer`, `measurement_defaults_restoration_stops_at_the_first_apply_error`, `raw_group_limit_stops_every_later_call`, `the_raw_group_deadline_is_checked_between_two_device_calls`, `raw_group_restores_after_a_panic_in_it`, `a_failed_restore_leaks_the_handle_even_when_the_record_s_writing_panics`.
  `group_run_keeps_the_other_groups_after_an_error_or_a_panic`, `the_record_is_assembled_from_an_identity_read_before_the_groups_with_no_device`, `a_probe_s_long_arrays_are_summarised_and_their_rows_go_to_a_side_file`, `ur_7_retries_only_the_reference_clock_not_locking`, `rejected_runs_are_admission_rejections_not_group_failures`, `frequency_sweep_covers_both_ends_and_mixes_whole_and_off_mhz_points`, `frequency_values_need_every_point_within_a_twentieth_of_a_hertz`, `provider_measurement_groups_fake_rehearsal_round_trips_without_claiming_measurements` and, in `ezsdr-radio`, `rm_35_a_measured_frequency_sweep_passes_or_fails_the_profile_grid_by_its_ends_and_step`.
  The record's layout, image and links come from `EZSDR_MEASUREMENT_SELECTOR`: a JSON object of `rx`, `tx`, `image`, `links` and `link_gbps` only, giving both `rx` and `tx` or neither (the other would be inherited from the device), refused before the device opens and laid over every profile the record's groups assemble; absent, the layout is the device's slot A. UR-5 and RM-28 judge whether it fits the device, and a layout that does not is the group's failure at its first Run. A layout entry's `#<name>` (RM-28) is not part of the board: the raw probes' slot-A test reads the entry before it. Each channel runs at its front end's default frequency, and the RF envelope has a band and a `tx_enabled` entry for each transmit channel (RM-33).
  *Test:* `the_selector_environment_names_only_the_layout_image_and_links`, `an_installed_selector_keeps_its_layout_and_gives_each_channel_its_own_band`, `a_selector_the_device_does_not_fit_is_the_groups_failure`, `the_one_channel_transmit_spec_carries_transmit_channel_zero_s_own_frequency`.
  The raw probes drive slot A's channel 0 at 1 GHz. With a selector they run only when its receive and transmit channel 0, each when named, is slot A on a `ubx` or `obx` element; otherwise each is recorded as inapplicable to the layout, and none reaches the device.
  *Test:* `raw_probes_are_inapplicable_when_channel_zero_is_not_slot_a_on_a_ubx_or_obx`.
  A direction with two or more channels runs, beside the one-channel sweep, a stream of all its channels, a stream of each channel at one rate, and a stream of each channel with channel 0 at a grid rate and channel 1 at the next lower. No stage runs both directions. Each stage is judged stream by stream on its own samples, accepted sends and acknowledgements; a stage the candidate ceiling (`UhdRadio::with_measurement_performance`, one link's line rate in each direction) does not admit is an admission rejection, not a failure. Each stage's observation records the order its streamers opened and the byte counters of the NICs named by `EZSDR_MEASUREMENT_NICS`; a stream's number is not a physical link.
  *Test:* `two_channel_receive_throughput_runs_the_grouped_split_and_mixed_conditions`, `two_channel_transmit_throughput_runs_the_grouped_split_and_mixed_conditions`, `a_starved_stream_fails_its_stage_and_the_others_still_show_success`, `a_partial_send_fails_the_transmit_stage`, `a_burst_acknowledgement_that_never_comes_leaves_the_stream_incomplete`, `one_unacknowledged_channel_of_a_stream_leaves_it_incomplete`, `a_duplex_stage_fails_when_the_companion_direction_does_not_flow`, `a_stage_above_the_admission_ceiling_is_an_admission_rejection_not_a_failure`, `a_stage_between_the_profile_limit_and_the_candidate_ceiling_is_admitted`, `the_candidate_ceiling_admits_what_the_profiles_link_limit_refuses`, `stage_lists_follow_the_layout`, `a_stage_is_as_bad_as_its_worst_stream` and `the_candidate_ceiling_is_one_links_line_rate_per_direction`.
  `radio.perf.rx_bytes_per_s` and `radio.perf.tx_bytes_per_s` are the largest bytes per second T such that every condition with a stage at a T' of at most T succeeded there. A condition with no stage at a T does not judge it; a stage that did not run, was rejected or did not succeed stops the climb; none succeeding leaves the key `unmeasured`. The row's condition is one that succeeded at T — the stage of the stopper's own family and layout (rate aside) that passed at T, under its actual id, else the first that passed there; the stopper is recorded as the blocker, apart. A stage that fails (its `Err` or a panic) is `not run`, ends the climb and is recorded with its error; the stages before it and the one-channel sweep keep their rows.
  *Test:* `capacity_of_one_condition_is_the_successful_prefix_of_the_sweep`, `capacity_over_conditions_judges_each_t_by_the_conditions_that_have_it`, `the_capacity_names_a_condition_that_passed_and_the_blocker_apart`, `a_stage_that_fails_ends_the_climb_and_keeps_the_stages_before_it`, `a_stage_that_wedges_the_handle_stops_the_group_and_caps_the_capacity`.
  Once both throughput values are decided, one pair runs for the throughput duration: the receive streams of the condition that held the receive value and the transmit streams of the one that held the transmit value, each on its own channels and rate, at once. It decides no value; its observation records each stream's evidence, how much of the duration transmit flowed and the stretch in which the two overlapped, and says whether the two values held at once; it is a single confirmation, not evidence for every pair the profile admits or a longer run. With either value `unmeasured` it is not run, and the observation says why.
  *Test:* `the_duplex_pair_runs_each_direction_on_the_streams_that_held_its_value`, `the_duplex_pair_is_not_run_without_both_values_and_says_so`, `the_duplex_pair_runs_the_two_measured_limits_together_once_and_decides_no_value`.
  The overflow gap has a companion group of its own, with no value key, that runs a 10 MS/s stream of the other direction; it is skipped, with an observation, when the layout lacks that direction. It is an observation of what that Run did (the Run's samples moved and its faults, not attributed to its trials) and decides no value, so a failure of it is that group's alone and the solo group's values stay.
  *Test:* `a_companion_that_fails_after_a_solo_measurement_keeps_the_solo_value`, `a_companion_group_is_skipped_with_an_observation_when_the_layout_lacks_the_opposite_direction` and `the_overflow_gap_adds_a_condition_with_the_other_direction_running`.
  The waveform of every transmission the duplex stages and the companions add has a complex magnitude of at most 0.5, asserted before it is sent.
  *Test:* `the_waveforms_of_the_added_transmissions_stay_within_half_a_unit`.
  Every device handle of the record is a `GuardedDevice` on one latch, and the record opens each through `guard_open` or `open_record_guard`, which call no opener once the latch holds. The latch holds on an `apply` error, on a control timeout or a device loss from any call (a failed `rx_recv` included), on an open of channels that another open stream of the same direction still holds (a re-open of the same stream replaces its entry and is forwarded, as `UhdDevice` drops the old streamer first), and on a streamer left open after a group. From then on every call that would reach the device is refused without reaching it, the guard's description, master clock and fidelity having been read when it was made, and the groups after it are recorded as not run. A latched guard leaks its handle when dropped, unwinding included. The record opens, describes and identifies the device and closes it before the raw probes, and opens it again for the groups unless a probe latched the record.
  *Test:* `nothing_reaches_the_device_after_a_control_timeout`, `nothing_reaches_the_device_after_a_prepare_time_apply_failure`, `nothing_reaches_the_device_after_a_group_leaves_a_streamer_open`, `an_open_of_channels_another_open_stream_holds_is_refused_before_the_device`, `a_reopen_of_the_same_stream_and_a_disjoint_channel_set_are_allowed`, `a_forwarded_receive_timeout_or_loss_latches_and_blocks_later_calls`, `a_latched_guard_reads_nothing_from_the_device_and_leaks_it_when_dropped`, `a_latched_record_calls_no_opener` and `the_guard_latches_on_apply_errors_timeouts_and_losses_only`.
  After the raw group, the record reopens only when its latch is clear, the device lease is clean and the lease is the only strong `Arc` owner. A skipped or failed reopen leaves its applicable RX-throughput values `unmeasured`; successful reopened values combine with the earlier values by minimum throughput.
   *Test:* `record_reopen_requires_a_clear_latch_clean_lease_and_exclusive_owner`, `restore_failure_and_unwound_group_refuse_reopen_without_opening_again`, `reopened_plan_halves_counts_rounding_up_and_never_zero`, `reopened_receive_throughput_repeats_only_the_single_channel_sweep`, `reopened_values_use_the_conservative_pair_and_failures_invalidate_base_values`.
  The receive start-lead measurement group, which measured Run-time rate transitions, is deleted until note 47 redesigns it: `radio.timing.rx_start_lead_ns` has no group in the record and no measured value, and `radio.timing.tx_start_lead_ns` no longer exists. The H4 control-dispatch observation is retired; its TX headroom and load measurement stays.
   The `hw_h4_load` probe is separate from the raw probes and uses the selected profile's channel 0. It asks 10 MS/s on receive and transmit channel 0 at connect, starts a TX repeat and sends no timed update. It records an observation only: the TX headroom proxy is not RF execution time. TX underflows are converted through the Manifest's SampleClock records; updates after TX ends are excluded from the concurrent distribution, and an interrupted TX makes the result partial.
   *Test:* `hw_h4_load`, `h4_mapping_gives_an_ordered_interval_and_refuses_bad_anchors`, `h4_mapping_rejects_a_monotonic_forward_clock_jump`, `h4_tx_headroom_uses_per_channel_cumulative_accepts_and_breaks_bad_segments`, `h4_tx_headroom_restarts_after_an_error_and_eob`, `h4_tx_headroom_discards_a_discontinuous_host_device_mapping`, `h4_zero_sample_send_breaks_the_headroom_segment`, `h4_proc_parsers_retain_cpu_and_thread_policy_fields`, `probe_rows_stop_at_the_limit_and_count_dropped_rows` and `h4_load_self_test_reaps_pid_owned_workers_and_checks_the_memory_budget`.
- **UR-35** `build.rs` does nothing unless `CARGO_FEATURE_UHD` is set; then it links `uhd` from `UHD_LIB_DIR`, else from `pkg-config --variable=libdir uhd`, and fails with a message naming both ways when neither finds it. It adds no rpath.
  *Test:* none named for the link itself: the `UHD_LIB_DIR`/pkg-config search, the failure message naming both ways, and the absence of an rpath; the default build without the feature pins only that `build.rs` does nothing without `CARGO_FEATURE_UHD`.

## 5. Decisions

| # | Decision | Choice | Rejected (one line each) | Ceiling / upgrade path |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | The device boundary | One trait, `Device`, with ticks only; two implementations (UR-2, UR-3, UR-33) | Calling the C API from the Provider directly (no test without hardware); a trait per direction (the Authority and both directions share one device) | A second backend (SoapySDR, a C++ shim) implements the same trait |
| U2 | Threads | One per stream, plus control — control (books Actions, never waits for a device instant), each receive stream's uhd-rx and each transmit stream's uhd-tx (design 33 D15: one thread per stream, each owning its stream's streamer and performing its own switches; a tx thread also reads the asynchronous reports) — and one in the Authority, `uhd-clock` (UR-3, UR-7, UR-14…UR-28) | One thread doing everything (a blocking `send` would stall the receive path and the Actions); a separate async thread (the first draft: it shares the transmit streamer, whose reopen it would race — Review J, P1-7); switches on the control thread (it would block later Actions beyond the delivery allowance, P1-7) | none |
| U3 | `tx.repeat` | Streamed from host memory (UR-22) | RFNoC Replay (§35: device memory, alignment, a graph; no Phase 7 need) | Replay as a capability in a later version |
| U4 | `now()` | Extrapolated from an anchor re-read every 100 ms by `uhd-clock` and at every wakeup nap (UR-7) | Reading the device at every `now()` (a control-port round trip per call, and the coordinator calls `now` often); re-anchoring only in wakeups (the first draft: between client calls the extrapolation runs on the host clock alone, and at UR-8's own 1e-4 bound twenty idle seconds use up the 2 ms device lead — Review J, P1-4) | none |
| U5 | When the device's time is set | When the Authority is built, before the Run (UR-7) | At `arm` (the Run's T0 would depend on a PPS wait inside `arm`, and the root would not exist during `validate`) | Multi-device sets time on every device at one PPS |
| U6 | `cold` changes | A channel's frequency, gain and antenna are `cold` in both directions and staged by RM-21 (note 47 stages 3 and 4); a rate and stream membership have no update class | Run-time change of a rate or of membership (note 47 stage 2) | a rate change waits for a bench confirmation on a held-open streamer (note 47) |
| U7 | A transmit in-flight window | §3's in-flight window, with the hand-over horizon it implies stated (RM-15, RM-16; UR-21, UR-23) | Unbounded (a `Stop` would leave seconds on air); none (underflows); the window with the preemption unstated (the first draft: a burst preempting a repeat within it would be dropped by the device as late while its record claimed it on time — Review J, P0-2) | Phase 8 measures the least window without underflow |
| U13 | Timed commands | Withdrawn (note 47 stage 2): no key is changed during a Run (UR-24) | — | — |
| U14 | Enabling a stream at runtime | Withdrawn (note 47 stage 2): the channel set is fixed at connect (RM-21) | — | — |
| U8 | Alignment failures | A whole-stream gap qualified by `ALIGNMENT`, and `ALIGNMENT_ERROR` (UR-19) | A per-channel break (UHD never delivers part of a channel set) | none |
| U9 | Which errors mean a lost device | IO, USB, OS and RUNTIME errors on a streaming call, `EXCEPT` at the two stream opens, and 1 s of silence while streaming (UR-29) | Every error (a transient `recv` error would abort the Run); none (the Run would go on without its device) | B9 checks; a later version refines the list |
| U10 | Values and versions | One `x3xx` profile whose device values are `ezsdr_radio::x3xx`'s, with two Providers contributing only their own terms (UR-9, design 33 D5) | A copy of the X3xx values per Provider (two copies that drift apart); a profile claiming a verified envelope | Phase 8 measures; versions follow AGENTS.md §6 |
| U11 | Sample formats | `fc32` on the host, `sc16` on the wire (UR-3) | `sc16` on the host (the Radio Model's receive contract is `ezsdr.stream.cf32`, RM-2; a conversion Processor would be needed); `sc8` (halves the transport and the precision; §34 makes it a PerformanceEnvelope choice for later) | A wire-format selector key later |
| U12 | Instances and devices | One fragment per instance, one device per Run (UR-12; overview T13) | Two resources on one USRP (the layout's streams share one device and its rate set) | Multi-device in Phase 8b, after the parity test (Vision §67) |

## 6. Tests

`crates/ezsdr-radio-uhd/tests/fake.rs` runs every case through the real coordinator (`coordinator::start_spec_run` or `connect`) with an Assembly built as the server's catalogue builds it (the capture Sink, the host Link, `DeviceAuthority` and `UhdRadio` on one `FakeDevice`), unless the row says "unit".

The smoke set runs on the **manual** host clock (plan 30 §4.4): the device's time stops while a Provider thread is busy (M1–M7), so host load cannot move it past a deadline — whether that is enough for the mutation shards' load is the close's stress runs to measure. The test-side waits are that same clock (`device.host().sleep`) and the assertions that a Run met a device instant are measured on it; a wall-clock bound remains only as a watchdog, so a test that freezes or deadlocks fails instead of hanging the suite (plan 30 §7): every manual-clock test arms one shared watchdog (`tests/common/watchdog.rs`, included by `tests/fake.rs`, `crates/ezsdr-acceptance/tests/uhd.rs` and `crates/ezsdr-server/tests/protocol.rs`) that aborts the process with the test's name once a generous bound passes, and whose failure to start fails its test at once (`a_watchdog_that_cannot_start_fails_its_test`) (120 s by default, below the mutation runner's 180 s timeout; `EZSDR_TEST_WATCHDOG_SECS` raises it, which the load probe does — under oversubscription a test legitimately runs far longer). No short wall interval decides a functional property: where one needs the device's clock to stand still, a participant holds it until the test releases it after its checks (R19–R21).

A test whose point is a real host latency keeps the real clock (plan 30 §4.4): the Authority's own wake-latency diagnostics (`ur_07_schedule_wakes_a_waiting_next_wakeup` and `ur_07_cancel_wakes_a_waiting_next_wakeup`, their medians the only short wall verdicts left; the wakes themselves are pinned on a held clock by unit tests), the monotonic guard across a re-anchor (`ur_07_now_is_monotonic_across_a_reanchor`, whose bracket error is real time's), the intentionally wedged thread, and `ur_33_a_real_fake_s_time_starts_at_its_own_creation` (a device's time measured against the process-wide real clock, R07). With this clock the smoke set no longer proves that the Provider catches up with real time — that is Phase 8's bench work (plan 30 §4.6) — and the device-tick envelopes are unchanged.

These end-to-end tests are a smoke set of the integration — threads, streamers, device commands, read-back, reports, the transmit path, the bench rehearsals —; the timing rules are pinned deterministically by `rm_26_the_timeline_cases` and spec 22's three VH-8 layers, the last two (`rm_26_the_timeline_against_uhd_control`, `rm_26_the_timeline_against_uhd_rx`) on the `ManualTimeAuthority` rig, and the receive smoke test asserts on the plan `Provider::stop` records.

The HostClock's rules are pinned by `src/host.rs`'s unit tests, one per rule and each with a wall-clock bound: `m1_a_participant_registers_before_it_runs_and_unregisters_on_a_panic` and `m1_a_thread_that_never_starts_leaves_no_registration` (M1, R08), `m2_m3_a_busy_participant_stops_the_clock` (M2, M3), `m4_a_deadline_stops_the_clock_until_the_waiter_runs`, `m4_two_sleepers_stop_the_clock_at_the_earlier_deadline_and_go_on_to_the_later`, `m4_two_sleepers_with_one_deadline_stop_the_clock_there_until_both_run` and `m4_a_notify_racing_the_deadline_does_not_move_the_clock_past_it` (M4), `m5_the_clock_runs_at_real_speed_from_its_last_commit` and `m5_a_participant_going_idle_restarts_the_clock_from_that_instant` (M5, checked on the clock's state; `m5_the_clock_is_never_faster_than_real_time` and `m5_the_clock_does_not_catch_up_after_a_busy_interval` are the whole path's diagnostics),
`m5_a_transition_delayed_before_the_state_mutex_does_not_make_the_clock_jump` (M5, R02), `m6_a_non_participant_wait_stops_nothing` (M6), `m7_a_notify_does_not_end_a_sleep_early` (M7), `notify_a_notified_participant_is_busy_until_it_runs` (M4, R01), `wait_a_notify_ends_a_plain_wait_while_a_participant_is_busy` and `lock_a_waiter_waits_while_the_holder_sleeps_on_the_clock` (§4.7), `hand_off_the_clock_does_not_advance_after_the_lock_hand_off` (§4.7's hand-off), and `cpu_a_far_wait_sleeps_through_other_participants_cycles` with `cpu_a_stopped_clock_costs_a_near_wait_no_wakes` (the CPU measure, §5 of the plan: a wake storm and polling each pass).
A thread registered with `drive()` is pinned by the `m1_drive_…` tests of the same module: it is idle only while it waits on the clock it drives and a non-participant of every other clock, whichever clocks it drives and in whatever order they are dropped; a second registration on a manual clock is refused before it allocates; a panic or a drop removes its own registration only; a real clock registers nothing.

`src/authority.rs` pins the Authority on that clock: `ur_07_the_first_anchor_is_exact_on_the_manual_clock` and `ur_07_a_first_read_that_is_not_exact_refuses_the_authority` (UR-7: the first anchor is `uhd-clock`'s first read, exact, with no exception for `new`'s own read), `ur_07_a_read_bracketed_wider_than_1_ms_is_discarded` (UR-7's 1 ms rule; the Authority is built on a held clock so that its reads turn slow only after the first), `ur_07_a_manual_schedule_wakes_a_waiting_next_wakeup` and `ur_07_a_manual_cancel_wakes_a_waiting_next_wakeup` (MA-29 as KG-3 amends it; the waiter naps on a held clock, so only the wake ends its nap — rows U05, U34), `ur_07_dropping_the_authority_ends_uhd_clock_while_the_manual_clock_is_stopped` (UR-7's D-1, and the drop's promptness pin: the clock is held from before `uhd-clock` starts until it has ended, its `Weak` gone, so a drop that does not wake it leaves it waiting and detached), `ur_07_a_host_monotonic_wait_returns_while_a_participant_is_busy` and `ur_07_a_host_deadline_reaches_a_waiting_next_wakeup_while_a_participant_is_busy` (UR-7: a `host.monotonic` wait and schedule are judged on real host time — each ends while the clock is held, at or after its instant, the root standing still),
`ur_07_the_root_fires_first_when_both_are_due` and `ur_07_in_past_is_judged_per_root_timeline` (the two timelines), `ur_08_the_manual_clock_s_relations_hold_only_at_the_instant_measured` and `ur_08_the_host_domain_follows_real_time_across_a_busy_interval` (UR-8), `ur_07_the_host_queue_keeps_its_order_across_a_reanchor`, and `cpu_a_stopped_clock_with_a_near_root_entry_does_not_poll` (a `next_wakeup` on a stopped clock blocks, it does not poll); and the same rules on the real clock in `ur_07_the_host_timeline_is_judged_on_real_time_on_the_real_clock`.

`src/device.rs` pins the fake's raw receive cases (UR-33), each scenario's threads all participants of the manual clock so that its device instants do not depend on the host's load (plan 30 D2): `ur_33_a_stopped_stream_s_tail_comes_before_the_next_stream`, `ur_33_a_stopped_stream_s_recv_waits_its_whole_timeout` and `ur_33_a_recv_cut_short_by_a_packet_s_timeout_returns_what_it_has`; the same clock pins the boundary a call on a stream with no streamer meets: `ur_33_a_recv_on_a_stream_with_no_streamer_waits_its_10_ms`, `ur_33_a_report_read_on_a_stream_with_no_streamer_waits_its_timeout` and `ur_33_a_packet_size_read_of_a_stream_with_no_streamer_is_zero`. Four end-to-end tests use a test-local Device wrapper that deschedules a Provider thread for a real interval (plan 30 §7): `ur_21_a_descheduled_timed_send_is_on_time_on_the_manual_clock_and_late_on_the_real_one` (a near start, on time on the manual clock and late on the real one), `ur_26_the_shutdown_s_last_burst_ack_waits_on_the_device_s_clock` (R02) and `ur_34_the_receive_stall_fires_after_its_device_clock_interval` (S02, UR-34).

## 7. Vision coverage

| Vision | This spec |
|---|---|
| §35 the UHD bridge: typed status, exceptions translated, async errors as events, `DEVICE_LOST`, in-process | UR-2, UR-3, UR-28, UR-29 |
| §13 rules 1–2: the envelope published and enforced by the Provider on hardware | UR-9, UR-21, UR-24 |
| §15 the device timekeeper as Authority | UR-6, UR-7, UR-8 |

## 9. Vision issues found

1. **Resolved:** Vision §35 now says UHD's own C API (`uhd.h`) is such a narrow bridge: every entry point catches the exceptions UHD throws to it and returns a status, and only POD types and opaque handles cross it, so "write our own C++ shim" does not read as required (UR-2, UR-3, the `Device` trait).

## 10. Deferred

A second device per Run and its relation (Phase 8b, Vision §67). A USRP2 profile (after B9's `pp_string`). RFNoC Replay, DDC/DUC and FFT capabilities, and `extensions.uhd.rfnoc.*`. GPIO as a sub-resource (Vision §67's advanced peripherals, after the freeze; Vision §39). A wire-format selector (`sc8`, `sc12`). Refining the lost-device error list and the INFERRED leads from the bench (Phase 8). The removal of `UhdDevice::rx_recv`'s per-call allocations, which exist because the `Device` trait returns owned samples, measured by a copy-regression benchmark of the receive path (Phase 8; UR-32).

UR-15, UR-17 and UR-25 name stream 0 (`rx_start(0, T0)`, `rx_recv(0, …)`, `rx_packet_samples(0)`), the one stream a direction has until a later stage gives every stream its own. The fake's subdev markup splits on ASCII spaces alone and its three-part-token failure is `uhd_subdev_spec_make`'s (`subdev_spec.cpp:19-20,58-59`); a timed command's destination is the front end it was booked on — UHD's timed commands are the radio block's —, so a spec change moves it with its front end or drops it, and the fake applies it to that front end's channel or to nothing; a direction switched to 0 channels is the one caller that opens a streamer with no channel list (`tx_open(0, &[])`), which UHD reads as channel 0 (`sanitize_stream_args`) — `UhdDevice` hands the C API channel 0 there, where HEAD handed it a zero-length array —, and the fake does too, so a streamer opened with none is channel 0's; the fake's empty-spec and repeated-pair refusals are stricter than UHD 4.10's RFNoC `set_rx_subdev_spec`, which checks neither (`multi_usrp_rfnoc.cpp:2796`); a front end that stays keeps its settings because UHD's channels are the radio blocks', whose registers a channel map does not touch (INFERRED); `serial` reads channel 0's `mboard_serial`, which a device with no receive channel would refuse (INFERRED); `tx_send` refuses a buffer count or length its streamer does not have, as `UhdDevice` does before its C call; `UhdDevice` frees its subdev spec handle on a refused `uhd_subdev_spec_make` too, since UHD allocates the handle before it parses the markup (`subdev_spec_c.cpp:31-36`); a fake call on a stream no streamer is open for waits — `rx_recv` at most 10 ms of its host clock and `tx_async` its timeout — and then fails or finds no report, where `rx_start`, `rx_stop` and `tx_send` fail at once, and `UhdDevice` fails or finds none at once in every one; the wait is the part the M-rules need (plan 30 §4.7). The tests of `ur_33_two_receive_streamers_keep_their_own_channels` and `ur_33_setting_a_spec_disconnects_that_direction_s_streamers` grew, with the new `ur_33_an_empty_channel_list_is_channel_zero` and ur_33_a_timed_command_follows_its_front_end_across_a_spec (renamed untimed_front_end_value_follows_the_spec by note 48 S2a-i-β), to pin both directions' disconnection, the empty list and the destination (fix round 1: Sol 1–6, Opus O1–O3). Fix round 2: a spec change settles the commands due up to it under the spec it is leaving, so a command due while its front end was out is dropped and the channel that front end returns on starts at its defaults (INFERRED: how UHD's own radio block treats a command that came due while the block was out of the channel map is not measured; BP0's list covers the spec's machinery), and one not yet due at the return still applies (ur_33_a_command_due_while_its_front_end_was_out_never_applies, unit; removed with the timed queue by note 48 S2a-i-β); the fake's answer to a call naming a stream with no streamer is pinned on the manual clock — `rx_packet_samples` 0, `rx_recv`'s 10 ms wait before `Failed`, `tx_async` waiting out its timeout (`ur_33_a_packet_size_read_of_a_stream_with_no_streamer_is_zero`, `ur_33_a_recv_on_a_stream_with_no_streamer_waits_its_10_ms`, `ur_33_a_report_read_on_a_stream_with_no_streamer_waits_its_timeout`, all unit); `UhdDevice`'s subdev spec handle is freed exactly once on the accepted, refused-make and failed-set paths alike, counted by the make, set and free calls replaced for `src/uhd.rs`'s lib test binary (`uhd_a_subdev_spec_is_freed_once_on_every_path`), and `uhd_api_a_refused_subdev_spec_is_freed_once`'s description states only what it sees against real libuhd, since `uhd_subdev_spec_free` stores NULL and a second free through its slot deletes nothing (`subdev_spec_c.cpp:41`) (fix round 2: Sol 1–3) | [Phase 8 design 33](../plan/phase8/33-channels-and-streams.md) §5 stage 1b-4 |
